                               THE END OF AN ERA
                               - by GrayBark;;-"-
            In September, 1953, a new type of publication appeared. It pre­
sumed to report on sightings of flying saucers, and wa:; called "The Saucerian�
            "The Saucerian" was a labor of love. The first three issues were
cranked out on a hand ditto machine by the editor. Since the ditto process
will print only about 200 copies clearly, that was the limit of our circula­
tion. Soon people, by some way or another, heard of the publication and sub­
scribed to it. The subscription list soon grew so that we were forced to have
offset printed editions made, at great expense. Circulation continued to grow
until the present total of around 1,500 was reached.
            "The Saucerian" (later called "The Saucerian Bulletin") always op­
erated at a great financial loss. In the early days these losses were made up
by putting in funds from my theatre film booking business. Later, when I gave
up most of my theatre accounts in order to spend more time at "saucering,"
"The Saucerian" was supported by the sale of books about flying saucers.
            In the past three years a great new interest has a.l.m.ost obsessed
me - that of bringing out hard cover and paper cover books which tell the sau­
cer story far more completely than can brief articles in small magazines.
            For some time, "The Saucerian" has been published most irregular­
ly. Although subscriptions were sold with the full understanding to the sub­
scriber that it was an irregularly published magazine, the lack of frequency
of publication did annoy a great many subscribers, with good reason. With my
increasing interest in the publication of New Age books, I had very little
time to publish just sightings and to get together the elaborate magazine. Due
to ever-increasing costs, I knew I could not always afford to publish the
money-losing venture and still be able to bring out the more important Works.
Lately a number of happenings which may have been persecutions (although I
certainly cannot prove it), have led me to feel that I should concentrate on
book publication.
            Some time ago, James w. Moseley, hearing of some of my difficul­
ties, offered to combine 11 The Saucerian Bulletin" with his SAUCER NEtlS, and to
fill all of our subscriptions with this combined publication. He also assured
me that SAUCER N&IS would continue, even though a great many other saucer
zines were suspending publication. He also invited me to become an associate
editor with active participation in the supplying of material to the magazine,
although we agreed that I would not have the responsibility of fonning policy.
            Although Moseley and I had had a great many differences of opin­
ion and a "feud" in our publications, I respected him as a capable editor, es­
pecially since, in the past two years, his editorial policy has become more
sensible and liberal. I had thought of combining my publication with some
other in order to give "The Saucerian Bulletin" greater strength and the guar­
antee of perpetuation, but frankly SAUCER N&IS was the only regularly publish­
ed saucer magazine I felt could continue indefinitely. Mr. Moseley is appar­
ently financially able to continue his publishing at great losses, and intends
to do so.
            Recently when Mr. Moseley dropped by Clarksburg, we talked this
matter over further, and when I was assured, both by belief and by legal doc­
uments that the combination of the two magazines would be advantageous to my
subscribers, I decided, finally, to cease publishing "The Saucerian Bulletin"
as a separate magazine. I believe that you will find SAUCER NEUS combined with
"The Sauceria.n Bulletin" the best fiying saucer magazine available today. I
shall attempt to help Mr. Moseley continue to make it so.
            With the cessation of "The Saucerian Bulletin" as a separate enti-
                                                                              19 .

ty, we have reached the end of an era. It has been a decade of great satis­
faction.
            But I feel that a new era has begun. I feel it is my mission in
the saucer field to publish full-length books about the subject. Books are
naturally more complete than periodicals, and carry a respect that mere maga­
zines cannot.
            Since we began our publishing activities in the book field, we
have brought you "From Outer Space to You," by Howard Menger; "Doctor from
Lhasa," by T. Lobsang Rampa; "My Contact with Flying Saucers" by Dino Kraspe­
don; and "The Sky People" by Brinsley le Poer Trench.
            Now that the responsibility of producing "The Saucerian Bulletin 11
rests on other very capable shoulders, a great weight has been lifted from me,
and I can go ahead at deeper and more meaningful UFO publishing. Mr. Moseley
                                                                        •
has consented to insert a list of my various books into each issue of his
magazine so that you will be familiar constantly with my work.

            SAUCER NEJIS EDITOR JOINS EXPEDITION TO NEW MEXICO CAVE& Not long
ago we received a letter from Mr. R. L. Dobbins, a resident of Magdalena, New
Mexico. In it he describes a most unusual expedition he has planned for early
June. He has invited your Editor to become a member. The letter from Mr. Dob­
bins reads in parts
            "There is a strange cave somewhere near this locale. Two discs the
size of car headlights have been seen coming from the cave each night, and go­
ing upward out of sight. Two unusual small doors have also been seen, back a
few feet, in the mouth of the cave. At the cave entrance, there is a hole
about twenty feet in diameter and two hundred feet deep. When the members of
the last expedition descended to a depth of twenty feet, there was such an ef­
fect upon the spine that no one was able to go down any further. Each individ­
ual's hair stood straight up and stayed that way until out of the cave. There
is something down there in the way of life. I have never seen a Dero, but I
would not be shocked if I were to see one•••••"
            Mr. Dobbins goes on to explain that members of the expedition will
be restricted to six. A !ive-day hike is anticipated. We have been assured
that we will be allowed to take photographs of whatever is seen, and to write
up the story fully for SAUCER NEWS afterwards. The whole story of this fasci­
nating trip will be told in the forthcoming September issue of our magazine.

            EXCLUSIVE REPORT ON SOUTH AMERICAN SAUCER 11 FLAP 11 1 As announced in
our two previous issues, a continuing saucer "flap" seems to be taking place
in South America, while things here in the United States are relatively quiet.
Due to our exclusive South American press clipping service, we are able to
keep you up to date, even though practically no mention of the South American
sightings has been made in U.S. papers. Here are the latest reports, translat­
ed by our Staff from Spanish-language newspaperss
            On the night of January 30th, more than � dozen people in Ica,
Peru, saw a bright-white luminous object traverse the sky. It was visible for
a period of five to ten minutes. Other sightings were made during January in
San Juan and other parts of Peru.
            Flying saucers seen near Paranas, Argentina, in January, are said
to have caused a shower of glass-like particles, which fell from the sky like
rain. Some of these strange particles were kept for analysis, and were found
to contain calcium, magnesium, and several other minerals.

             On the 4th of February, five photos of a UFO were taken in quick
succession by Adolfo del Valle Rivero, an engineering student living in Tucu­
man, Argentina. Two of the pictures (too poor in quality to reproduce here)
were published in the February 15th edition or "La Gaceta," a Tucuman news­
paper. The pictures were developed right in the newspaper's own laboratory,
and do not appear to be faked. They show what Rivero described as a saucer
flying at relatively low speed at a distance of a half mile or so from the
camera.
             A very strange occurrence is reported 1n the February 5th edition
of the same newspaper. A farmer named Ricardo Lopez, who lives a few miles
from Tucuman, reported having round on his property two circles about ten feet
in diameter. These are shown 1n the illustration above. (The dimensions are 1n
meters. One meter is about equal to one yard.) Lopez took reporters to the
scene, and it was found that the grass had been pressed down and subjected to
intense heat in the white areas shown in the diagram, - as if some giant iron
had been placed on top of it. It was calculated that a temperature of over
2,000 degrees must have been present, as the grass and soil were affected to a
depth of several inches. However, there was no evidence of actual fire. It was
also ealculated that the circles had been made about two weeks before being
discovered by the farmer. No explanation for them could be found.
             In and around Arica, the northernmost city of Chile, a great many
sightings were made during January. On the night of January 2Jrd, a truck
traveling with ten passengers from Chapiquina to Arica was halted by a huge
blue ball or fire which seemed to be coming straight toward it. The passengers
in the rear fled 1n panic, whereas those in the cabin remained where they
were. The UFO came to a stop at low altitude precisely over the truck. The
witnesses described the object as being cigar-shaped, with a white cabin and
with flames coming out from the tail.
             On January 28th, a large number of people in the city of Arica
were panicked by a group of UF0 1 s "with dark cabins," which were hanging mo­
tionless over the town at low altitude. That same night, a truck traveling be­
tween Mamina and Iquique, driven by a former officer of the Chilean Air Force,
came upon two flying saucers which remained stationary above the vehicle for a
period of more than ten minutes.
             About January 29th, a saucer was photographed by an Arica mer­
chant named Gino Rossi. This photo is reproduced at the top of the next page.

              NEWS BRIEFS: In
Piove di Sacco, Italy,a young
painter named Mario Sal.maso
claimed last February to have
photographed a flying saucer and one of its occupants. He says he was walking
alone through snow-covered woods when he saw a huge, round luminous object,
near which was standing a man in an outfit which appeared to be made of cello­
phane. Sa.l.maso took four pictures while the man climbed into the saucer and
took off. Unfortunately, we have not seen any of these pictures, which must be
quite sensational if they are genuine•••••A strange glowing object, which may
have been a meteor, was seen in many parts of Long Island on the night of
March 25th. One witness described it as "a circular object that appeared like
a flourescent greenish light." It seemed to throw off white fire streaks from
a tail as it disappeared behind a cloud and then reappeared. The object was
also seen as far away as Norfolk, Virginia ••••Wilbur Smith, the former head of
a semi-official Canadian saucer research program, died last December. It is
rumored that Smith, who was originally a scientifically-minded UFO investi­
gator, turned contactee in his later years••••
            A UFO was seen near Washington, D. C. on the night of February 6th
by two men flying a private plane, and visual confirmation was made by another
plane, though nothing showed up on Washington radar. Carl Chambers of South
Williamsport, Pa., told reporters that he and another man with him saw the ob­
ject for nearly an hour, while flying from Charlottsville, Va., to Washington.
The UFO appeared off their right wing at a distance of several miles, and
moved toward, under and above the aircraft. It hovered awhile, and later went
about 75 miles in 5 seconds. The object seemed to have a ball-like shape, and
Chambers estimated it to be about ten feet in diameter. The light from it was
so bright that it cast a reflection on the plane••••From all we have read, the
results of the recent Venus probe by Mariner II appear to be inconclusive and
subject to conflicting interpretations. The rocket passed within 21,000 miles
of Venus last December 14th, and gave American scientists a great deal of new
data about that mystery planet. The surface temperature is now thought to be
about 800 degrees, too hot for life as we know it; but according to Homer E.
Newell, director of space sciences for the National Aeronautics and Space Ad­
ministration, there is a possibility that micro-organisms live in the atmos­
phere of Venus. There is also a possibility (in our opinion) that much of the
data will turn out to be wrong, and that the surface of the planet still uy
be found to support intelligent 11.!e of some sort••••
             On March 12th, a UFO traveling west at a very high altitude and a
high rate of speed was sighted by hundreds of persons in the Hawaiian islands.
Two Hawaii Air National Guard pilots said they saw the object north of the is­
land of Kauai during a routine training mission in jet fighters, at an alti­
tude of 40,000 feet. Honolulu newspapers and radio stations were flooded with
calls••••A Protestant clergyman has stated that churches must plan now to deal
with the possible creation of "feeling and thinking beings" which may soon be
22.

created artificially by scientists working in laboratories. The clergyman's
statement came about as a result of the fact that scientists claim they are
approaching a breakthrough in the creation of life from inanimate matter••••
A "strange red ball of fire" was seen by Bill DeHaan of Bozeman, Montana, at
2:JO a.m. on February 21st, as he was driving home from work. DeHaan said that
the UFO shook and swayed his car as it went by, and that it landed about 200
feet from his vehicle. Sheriff's deputies spent the morning combing the area,
but found nothing••••••
            Dr. Donald H. Menzel, the noted anti-saucer astrophysicist from
Harvard University, who wrote a book called "Flying Saucers" in 1952, has been
on a lecture tour recently, apparently to drum up interest in a second book on
the same topic which he plans to publish soon. Dr. Menzel is still as anti­
saucer as ever•••••UF0 1 s were seen darting about at an altitude of 15,000 feet
over Maiden, North Carolina, on the morning of February 18th. Floyd Hester
told the local newspaper that they were moving in and out of formation at tre­
mendous speeds. He described them as metallic in appearance, and said they
were in view for 30 minutes. At times they would hover motionless in the sky,
and then would move with a burst of speed. They left no trail of smoke••••
"Comic Relief" Itemz Men and women who have claimed to see flying saucers met
recently for a discussion meeting to compare notes, in Bologna, Italy. They
could not agree on anything, the meeting turned into a battle, and the cafe
owner threw them all out when cups as well as saucers began flying in a battle
royal.
            "S.P.A.C.E. 11 AND TWO OTHER SAUCER PERIODICALS FOLD UP: Norbert
Gariety, editor of the monthly saucer magazine called "S.P.A.C.E.," has an­
nounced in his November 1962 issue (released in January, 1963) that he has de­
cided to throw in the towel. 11 S.P.A.C.E. 11 lasted for six years, and at one
time was about the best flying saucer zine in the United States, due to its
fine coverage of Fortean events as well as saucer sightings. But then Gariety
went off on a wild right-wing political "kick," and started filling his pages
with political diatribes of various sorts, - especially when sightings were
scarce. When he was told by your SAUCER NEl-lS Editor and many others that poli­
tics have no place in a saucer magazine, Gariety became increasingly bitter.
Now he has finally decided to devote all his spare time and energies to his
various political activities, and in view of everything, we feel that saucer
research will be better off without him. Gariety is so petty that he refused
to send us his final issue - though we had been receiving all the previous 70
issues on an exchange basis.
            Two other saucer periodicals· recently defunct are "The Hawk Re­
searcher," published by Harry Siebert of Pomona, California, and "The N.J.A.
A.P. Bulletin," published by F.dward J. Babcock, Jr., of Kendall Park, New Jer­
sey. The editors of both these magazines are teenagers with whom we have had
considerable correspondence. Both periodicals were improving in quality as
time went by; but as so often happens in cases such as these, the young edi­
tors were eventually moved to devote themselves to other activities, so that
their saucer research had to be cut down or abandoned. We hope that other cap­
able young men will enter the saucer field to take their place.
            FORTEAN ITEMSz The Smithsonian Institute recently set up an elabo­
rate system of cameras· and a spectroscope near the town of Maco, North Caro­
lina, in order to find the explanation for a mysterious flickering light that
has been seen in the area for generations. Hundreds of people have witnessed
the light over the years, but when approached too closely, it disappears. Un­
fortunately we do not have the results •of the Smithsonian's expedition••••Into
                                                                            2J.

the category of "mysterious disappearances" goes this story from Cinnaminson
Township, New Jersey. On March 18th a 2½ year old boy, who had been left for
only five minutes to play with his brother and cousin in the back yard, van­
ished without a trace. Since then, 500 policemen, state troopers, FBI agents
and volunteers have searched every inch of the southern New Jersey township
without resultsl ••• There is a haunted house in Nashville, Tennessee, where the
family of a resident named Jack Hawkins is disturbed every night around 6 p.m.
by a loud knocking of some sort. Repeated efforts by police and others to find
the source of the knockings have met with no success•••••Another recent ghost
story comes from Detroit, where the Ada.ms family of 5508 Martin St. bas aban­
doned their house due to "a formless hag with the cold staring eyes of death,
and gray hair strung like decayed Spanish moss over her indescribably horrible
face." Mrs. Lillian Adams says she heard its wall - a part human, part animal
moan that made her scalp tingle. Other members of the family have seen, heard,
or smelled the terrifying ghost, and the family is determined never to live in
the house again••••
            A Norwegian explorer says he has found positive evidence that the
Vikings settled Newfoundland, Canada, 500 years before Columbus landed in the
New World. Carbon 14 tests succeeded in dating a primitive forge, nails, and
other remains found at Lance aux Meadows on Newfoundland's north coast. The
tests put the date of the objects at about 1,000 A.D••••An article from Cleve­
land, dated March 7th, states that a 6-pound chunk of iron crashed through the
roof of the home of Mrs. John Maxwell, accompanied by a noise which sounded
like an airplane crash or a sonic boom. It was thought that the object could
have fallen from a plane, but cast iron is not generally used in aircraft
parts, and no plane could be found which might have lost a part over the Max­
well house at that time•••A scientific team investigating the famous Loch Ness
monster concluded last February that something big and alive does indeed in­
habit the Scottish lake. The head of the team said that there is no way yet to
determine whether it is a mammal, a reptile, an amphibian or a fish ••••Russian
scientists claim to have brought two prehistoric tritons (vertebrates resem­
bling lizards) back to life. The animals lay frozen solid for 5,000 years in
Siberia before being revived by the scientific team. According to the Russian
report, one of the animals lived, ran around, ate, and slept for three weeks
before dying, and the other was alive "practically all summer."
                                 BOOK REVllWS
                            - by 'Tsi'es W. Moseley -
(�: In spite of our recent merger with "The Saucerian Bulletin," we still
feel obliged to give our frank opinions about all current books, including
those published by Barker's organization.)
BENDER MYSTERY CONFIRMED -edited by Gray Barker (Published by Saucerian·Books)
            This book is a collection of letters to Gray Barker from a number
of people who read Albert K. Bender's "Flying Saucers and the Three Men: which
we reviewed in our March issue. Unfortunately, a better title would be "Bender
Mystery Confused," due to the fact that these letters add little to our under­
standing of the Bender book or to our understanding of the saucer mystery in
general. The information revealed in the letters ranges all the way from evi­
dence tending to confirm Bender's statements to evidence directly contradict­
ing them, - with a lot thrown in which neither confirms nor contradicts any­
thing.
            In his introduction, Barker tells us, "No effort has been made to
create a literary work, for the publication of letters carries the duty of
24.


presentation without editing, except in the most minor degree." It is unfor­
tunate that Barker did not follow his own rule about editing. Chapter 16, en­
tiUed "Were Bender's Experiences Psychological?" appears to consist of a long
letter from me, explaining "flying Saucers and the Three Men" in psychological
terms. Unfortunately, this chapter bears litUe relationship to the short let­
ter I sent Barker expressing my opinions on the Bender book.
                                                                             .
              One can only hope that the other letters in "Bender Mystery Con­
firmed" were edited less severely. Same of the supposedly confirmatory letters
are so strange in content that they leave litUe doubt that they are either
the result of Barker's "editing" or of severe mental disturbance on the part
of the letter writer. One letter, supposedly tending to confirm Bender's head­
aches, reads in parts "After my collapse, or rather during it, after my going
through reinoarnation••••my sun lamp lit up with a blue light, and to this day
I do not know if it is a healing ray or straight from hell •••••I kept hearing
that it was a station like a TV set and was hooked up to the police station,
where they could see my every move. I was the object of ridicule, torment, and
gutter filth. For days the many voices talked on, even after I broke the sun
lamp bulb••••• 11
              There are, however, more lucid letters than this. The consensus of
opinion (if there is any) among Barker's letter writers seems to be that Ben­
der is telling the truth as he knows it. But perhaps the alleged space people
were fooling him, by misrepresenting their true identity, or giving him false
information on some points. There is talk of as��al entities, the Serpent
Race, Daros, the Black Brotherhood, Communist agents, and other such groups.
One woman writes that upon reading the first part of "flying Saucers and the
Three Men, 11 she was "all for heaving the book into the fire� And, in fact, one
of Barker's promotion circulars is headlined "Should You Burn the Bender
Book???" No, in our opinion you should not; but many readers will surely do a
slow burn when they read its sequel.
!!IQ WARNING - by John Stuart (Published by Saucerian Books, Clarksburg, W.Va.)
            Although we have long suspected that there is often more than a
slight connection between flying saucers and sex, we were not sure about this
theory until reading John Stuart's "UFO Warning." Mr. Stuart, after being ex­
pelled from a New Zealand saucer club for allegedly misusing their stationery,
set up his own organization, called flying Saucer Investigators. This group
consisted of exactly two members - himself and an attractive young lady named
Barbara, with whom he spent most of his evenings discussing saucer sightings
and theories. Just what his wife may have thought about this research is not
clear, as she is barely mentioned in the book.
            One night a hideous green monster appeared on the lawn and appar­
enUy hypnotized both members of flying Saucer Investigators, so that they
were unable to move. Just as it seemed about to attack or rape the fem.ale half
of the organization, it changed its mind, moved away, and disappeared. Another
night, Barbara was raped by three invisible beings while alone in her room.
The invisible attackers left her body covered with strange scratches, which
she later showed to John Stuart. Shortly after this attack, Barbara threw in
the towel on flying saucer research and moved to another town. Thereafter,
says Stuart, "I went away to Auckland and stayed with my old mother, resting
my nerves, trying to bring my shocked system back into order."
            Our opinion of "UFO Warning" can best be expressed in the words of
Barker himself, in his introduction to this epic: "Although I cannot complete­
ly understand this volume, and I doubt if many others can either, it is a def­
inite part of the huge mass of confusing data which forms the UFO enigma."

            LANlllARKS IN THE HISTORY OF SAUCER NENSs Our magazine was begun in
July of 1954 under thenam.7"Nexus." h June oT'i955 the name was changed to
SAUCER m/S, and the publication schedule was changed from monthly to bi.­
monthly. In 1959 publication was put on a quarterly basis, and since that time
our magazine has appeared in March, June, September and December of each year.
            In early 1963 SAUCER NEWS was combined with Gray Barker's "Saucer�
ian Bulletin� This merger makes SAUCER NENS the largest and second oldest sau­
cer periodical in the world. Circulation is currently slightly under 4,000.
SAUCER NEWS is widely distributed throughout the United States as well as Eng­
land, Canada, and various parts of the British Em.pire.
            POLICY, Our official policy is that no final solution has yet been
found to the flying saucer mystery. We favor the theory that saucers come from
some other planet, but we feel that this has not yet been proven.
            However, contributors to our magazine are completely free to ex­
press their own opinions in their signed articles, regardless of whether or
not these opinions agree with those of the F.ciitors.
            INFORMATION CONCERNING MATERIAL SURUTTED TO SAUCER N»IS: We are
always in need of good material, and are glad to recei;; articlesand letters
from our subscribers. Preference is given to articles dealing with new and
previously unpublished aspects of the saucer mystery. Unfortunately, we are in
the same position as all other saucer zines, in that we cannot pay for materi­
al. - We reserve the right to reject any manuscript, and to cut and edit as we
see fit. However, no editing is ever done except in an attempt to make clearer
the meaning which the writer of the article or letter is trying tc!> convey.
            ADAMSKI EXPOSE ISSUE AND NON-SCHEDULED NEWSLETTER& In 1957 we
brought out a special issue of SAUCER tffl;fs, exposing the unproven claims of
oontactee George Adamski. A few copies of this long and carefully-documented
expose are still available. - Throughout the years, Confidential Newsletters
have been published, on a non-scheduled basis, in addition to the regularly­
scheduled issues of our magazine. These Newsletters are made available to sub­
scribers in good standing who want to be on the inside of the strange and baf­
fling behind-the-scenes events in saucer research. The Confidential Newslet­
ters usually contain material which we consider "too hot to• handle" in our
regularly-scheduled issues. For the price of $1.00 you can be placed on the
special Newsletter mailing list for an indefinite period of time.

        Bill Daut is in reality Reverend William Daut, the minister of a spiritualist

Melvin is safe and has come to no harm. He will let his family know where he is when he
can prove himself. He is under the protection of an older man. His decision to leave
home was pre-planned. He is doing what he wants to do •
        .Another psychic, named Irwin Greif, heard about the Ginsberg disappearance and
asked for a photograph to psychometrize. His findings were almost exactly the same as
those of Reverend Daut, with one exception: Irwin feels that the older person is a man
living in Arizona.
        There is a possibility that Melvin Ginsberg, wherever he is, may contact saucer
researchers in bis area. May we ask all our Newsletter subscribers, - particularly
those living in Arizona - to be on the lookout for our missing member!
         (�: As your Editor was out of town at the time the above events took place,
the information for this Newsletter was gathered together and written up by our Assist­
ant F.ditor, John J. Robinson, to whom we owe our thanks for a job well done.)

compliments, and what-have-you. Please send all mail to our New Jersey address

Code" number. This is a new device by means of which the Post Office hopes to
speed the delivery of mail. We would appreciate it if all our subscribers
would send us their "Zip Code" number as soon as possible. Theoretically, you
have already received it from your local post.man.

                             FLORIDA'S CORAL CASTLE
                             - by James'w:71oseley -
             On June 21st of this year, I gave a talk to our Discussion and
Lecture Committee about a man-made wonder in southern Florida which I have
visited twice and found to be most fascinating. Whereas Coral Castle is only
indirectly connected with the study of flying saucers, the lecture aroused so
much interest in our local group that I decided to write it up for SAUCER NEWS.
             Coral Castle is located on U. s. Highway No. One, about 25 miles
south of Miami. This is the highway that runs all the way out to Key West,
through one of the most scenic areas of the United States. Today, Route No.
One is a busy four-lane thoroughfare as it passes Coral Castle; but back in
the early 1920 1 s, when work on the Castle was begun, there was only a trail
running south from Miami, and the idea of connecting the Florida keys by road
was still only a dream.
             Coral Castle was built single-handedly over a period of 25 years
by an eccentric Latvian immigrant named Edward Leedskalnin. Ed chose this
sparcely-settled section of Florida because 11 he wanted to get away from the
world, 11 having been jilted by his sweetheart on the eve of their wedding day
in his native Latvia. Here on the edge of the Everglades he apparently felt he
was far enough away from people, and settled down on a small plot of land
which a generous neighbor permitted him to use. Working entirely�. he
built a house for himself from logs cut from the surrounding pine trees, and
from coral rock which he quarried on the premises. There began the strange and
unusual lifetime project he set for himself, interrupted only by his moving to
a larger plot of ground nearby, a few years later.
             At its present location, Coral Castle is set on a ten-acre tract.
The Castle proper is surrounded by an eight-foot wall made of huge blocks of
coral rock, each weighing several tons. The tower, which occupies one corner
of the Castle, is shown in the picture on the cover of this issue. It contains
more than 160 tons of coral rock. Each block making up this huge two-story
building weighs nine tons. The first floor was used by Ed as a workshop, and
the second floor housed his living quarters. During his lifetime, Ed would al­
low curious visitors to pass through the grounds of the Castle, and he even
took pleasure in showing them around. But no one was ever allowed into the
tower, and an air of mystery prevailed around these quarters.
             Behind Coral Castle's huge walls, Ed placed a great number of mar­
velous pieces of coral rock furniture - objects which he created from his fer­
tile imagination. There are rocking chairs weighing thousands of pounds, so
delicately balanced that they move at the touch of a finger. There are couches,
beds, chairs, and tables of all sizes and shapes, including one stone table
about fifteen feet long, hewn from solid coral rock into the shape of the
state of Florida. (See bottom photo on Page 7.) There is another table cut in­
to the shape of a heart, with a beautiful ever-blooming floral centerpiece
growing out of its center. Ed never forgot the girl who jilted him. He called
her his "sweet sixteen� and apparently went through life with the thought that
she might one day return to him. Much of the stone furniture within the Castle
was made in memory of the wife and family he never had; for there is a com­
plete bedroom, furnished with twin beds, a pair of children's beds, and a
cradle, all made of stone. There is also a patio for children to play in,
called "The Grotto of the Three Bears," containing all the furniture in the
famous fairy tale, including a huge coral porridge bowl. But Ed died in 1951
without ever marrying, having lived the life of a true hermit in his coral
wonderland.
             To enter Coral Castle, it is necessary to go through a "swinging
gate," - a triangular block of stone weighing three tons which turns with only
a slight push, permitting you to pass through. In the center of the rear wall
                                              there is a much larger stone gate
                                              weighing nine tons, reminiscent of
                                              ancient castles. This is shown in
                                              the picture at the top left. This
                                              tremendous hunk of coral is so per­
                                              fectly pivoted and balanced that a
                                              child can turn itl Also in the rear
                                              of the Castle is the huge stone ob­
                                              elisk, weighing twenty.eight tons,
                                              seen in the photo on Page 8. This
                                              obelisk has, within the circular
                                              hole near the top, two wires which
                                              cross each other at right angles,
                                              like a gunsight. The point where
                                              the wires cross lines up perfectly
                                              with a small hole in a rock facing
                                              the obelisk, and these in turn line
                                              up perfectly with the North Star.
                                              Ed was very interested in astronomy,
                                              and has coral representations of
                                              the moon and planets lining his
                                              rear wall. Ed believed that the
                                              other planets in our solar system
                                              are inhabited. It is not known for
                                              sure whether he believed that these
                                              extraterrestrial creatures are vis­
                                              iting us in fiying saucers, but it
                                              is likely that he did.
                                                      In my opinion Ed's greatest
                                              single achievement was his sundial,
                                              shown in the middle picture on this
                                              page. I have never seen or heard of
                                              another like it anywhere in the
                                              world. Without ever having had more
                                              than a secondary school education
                                              in his native Latvia, Ed was able
                                              to build this ingenious sundial
                                              which, when properly understood,
                                              gives the time correctly to within
                                              two minutes !J:1, year aroundl Ed
                                              read books and familiarized himself
                                              thoroughly with the apparent move­
                                              ments of the Sun, corresponding to
                                              the various seasons. Then he chart­
                                              ed the Sun's apparent course on a
         ........Ml.lllll1ti.'�-.,·--,        curved rock surface, as seen in the
                                              photograph.
               On December 21st, the date of the Sun's southernmost position, the
shadow of the pointer falls along the top of the loops; and on June 21st, the
date of the Sun's northernmost position, the shadow falls exactly along the
bottoms of the loops. To find out how near the tops of the loops the pointer
will fall on any given day of the year, it is only necessary to compute the
ratio of that date to the above-mentioned dates; but, as you can see in the
picture, the Sun's apparent motion is in a figure-eight rather than an oval
pattern, and the computations involved are rather complicated. However, to
tell time from this sundial, it is not necessary to understand these vertical.
movements of the shadow. The sundial. is read horizontally, in the following
8.
manners The loops shown in the photo are
numbered 10, 11, 12, l and 2. Those in be­
tween them are not numbered, but represent
10½, 11½, 12½ and l½- At any time of year,
the shadow of the pointer will fall within
the 10 loop between 10100 a.m. and 10:30
a.m; within the 10½ loop between 10130 and
11100 a.m; etc. In the picture, the point­
er is approximately in the middle of the 11
loop, so the picture was taken at about
11115 a.m. To obtain the time more accu­
rately, it would be necessary to measure
the distance of the pointer from the sides
of the loop.

fans, however, is the question of !l2! Ed

tools were found in the Castle after F.ci s  1
death; and as Ed refused to share his secret with anyone or to do any stone
moving when people were watching, - the secret of the construction of Coral
Castle died with the eccentric hermit who built it!
            When Ed Leedskalnin died, his Castle was looted, and perhaps some
key item that would have solved the mystery was lost forever at that time. The
looters missed F.ci 1 s treasure, however, - $3,000 in hundred dollar bills hidden
in a drainpipe in his bedroom. This was his life's savings, and consisted
mainly of money he had received from a relative 1 s oil business in Texas.
            In recent years the Castle has had added to it a modern souvenir
stand, and it is now a widely-advertised tourist attraction, with an admission
fee. The once-forbidden tower is now open to the public. But the Castle's air
of mystery remains the same as it must have been years ago, when only an occa­
sional visitor broke the hermit's solitude.
            Ed wrote several booklets during his lifetime and published them
at his own expense. The best-known is called "A Book in Every Home," and is
concerned only with the author's views on sex and politics. No mention at all
is made of the Castle. The other booklets are all about magnetism, which Ed
considered to be the key to the physical universe, and even to the operation
of the human body. It would be pleasant to tell you that F.ci 1 s experiments with
magnetism, which he describes in detail in these books, were the key to the
construction of Coral Castle. However, the experiments he wrote about were
relatively simple ones, involving automobile batteries, ordinary magnets, used
car parts, and other comm.on items which he obtained by rummaging through junk
yards. If Coral Castle was built by means of some secret discovery involving
electromagnetism, there is no hint of it in anything that F.ci published.
            We can therefore assume the most likely explanation to be that Ed
discovered new ways of using ordinary pulleys and levers. Whether this hypoth­
esis is true or not, I do believe that Ed 1 s claim to have re-discovered the
secret of the Pyramids was not an idle boast. The fact that this secret is un­
lmown to modern science ismerely one more proof that Mankind in the year
1963, on the threshold of space exploration, still has a great deal to learnt
                         GEORGE HUNT WILLIAMSON RE-VISITED
                              - by John J. Robinson -
               When I wrote the "I Go to See" articles for the "Journal of Cor­
relative Philosophy, 11 I gathered a great deal of information on various con­
tactees in the flying saucer field, some of which I never used. Much of this
material related to the claims made by George Hunt Williamson in Volume 29 of
11Who 1 s Who in America, 11 and in 11 American Men of Science� as well as the claims
made for him on the various book jackets and in the publicity releases to sell
these books, written by him.
               Some of the information below may be repetitive for long-time SAU­
CER N&lS readers. (See 11 Screwing the Lid Down on 'Doctor• Williamson 11 in our
February-March 1959 issue.) However, when Mr. Moseley informed me that a tour­
ist attraction such as Coral Castle contains some of these claims posted in a
prominent spot, I thought it time to review the case -      en -
                                                               toto. I therefore call
your attention to the following summary:
               Regarding any possible Doctorate having been awarded by Northwest­
ern University, Mr. Allen Weber of the University Counsel, Evanston, lliinois,
denied this in a letter over his signature, and asked for Williamson's address
so as to be able to enjoin him from making such claims.
               The University of Arizona has denied with vigor that any degrees
or awards were made to Williamson. They stated that he was disqualified for
poor scholarship in February of 1951 and had never been readmitted - in a let­
ter over the signature of David Windsor, Registrar and Director of Admissions,
University of Arizona, Tucson, Arizona, dated April 13, 1960.
               The claim in the "American Men of Science" listing as well as in
Williamson's book publicity, that he received the Gold Key of the Illinois
State Archaeological Society, has been discredited by Mr. William Smail of
Lazy Acre Farm, Loogootee, Illinois. Mr. Smail, a past president of the Soci�
ety, stated that only past presidents - all of whom he knew personally - ever
received the Gold Key. He denied that Williamson was a member of the Society,
and stated that he had never received the Gold Key, - in a letter over his
signature dated May 30th, 1960. At the same time, Mr. Ed Meiners, the then
current president of that Society, also denied the claim and stated that he
was communicating directly with Williamson's publisher regarding it, - in a
letter over his signature dated March 14th, 1960.
               The claim ma.de regarding membership in the American Association
for the Advancement of Science, has been cleared up by Mr. Hans Nussbaum,
Business Manager of the A.A.A.S., who stated in a letter from Washington over
his signature that the Williamson membership lapsed in 19.56.
               The Editor of "American Men of Science" stated that because of the
many inquiries, an investigation had been ma.de, and that Williamson's name
would be expunged from future editions. This was stated in a letter over the
signature of Jacques Cattel, Editor, "American Men of Science," Arizona State
University, Tenipe, Arizona.
               The National Anthropological and Archaeological Museum in Lima,
Peru, asserts that the National Archaeological Society of Peru has never seen
anything in regard to the Great Wall of the Incas which Williamson claims to
have discovered, nor, to be more specific, has the Peruvian government given
Williamson permission for any archaeological excavations. This letter was
written and signed by the secretary, Mr. George C. Muscle.
               In the light of all these letters, I am prone to believe that the
Great Western University of San Francisco, California (about which I can learn
nothing from accredited sources), is an off-shoot of the same Western Univer­
sity of San Diego which the Federal Trade Commission ordered to stop selling
degrees in Drugless Healing back in 1951. This massage parlor was also forbid­
den to use the word University.
10.
              It would seem to me, on the grounds that official scholarship does
not record this 11 University, 11 that the Department of Anthropology of the Great
Western University is a department of a "degree mill" and not that of a legal­
ly accredited institution. Our readers may remember that William.son has claim­
ed to be the head of this Department of Anthropology.
              Likewise, Dan Fry 1 s honorary Ph.D. degree - Doctor of Cosmology -
was awarded by the St. Andrews Ecumenical University College of London, which
is another degree mill, with connections with Aurea Publications of New York
State. Aurea Publications will deliver by mail a spiritualist minister's cre­
dentials, for a price.
              All of the above information was sent to me by people who do not
wish their names made known. However, a check will show all of these state­
ments to be the truth. Now for some fascinating information regarding William­
son's recent activities:
              In August of 1961, Mr. Takanashi, Editor of Japan 1 s "UFO Intelli­
gence" and a very good researcher, reported that William.son had voyaged to
Japan via the S.S. Sanuki Maru, on a passport bearing the name L. Obrenovic.
Since it is illegal to falsify a passport, one wonders about this! It was re­
ported that Williamson explained that since Obrenovic was hard to pronounce,
he used the name Williamson in every-day life.
              We do, however, have some additional information about this name,
for it was first used in connection with a wedding ceremony in San Louis Obes­
po, California, shortly before January 1st, 1961. On that date the Telegram
Tribune of the above-mentioned town carried the story that H.R.H. Prince Mich­
el d 1 0brenovic-Obilic van Lazar, Duke of Sumadya, had attended a wedding cere­
mony with his wife and his son Marc, of Paris. The story further reported that
the Prince was a noted anthropologist who wrote under the pen name George Wil­
liamson.
              A check with Yugoslav government officials has disclosed that a
royal family named Obrenovic existed until the year 1903. In that year the
head of the family was assassinated by revolutionists, and no living descend­
ants are known to exist.
              It is possible that George Hunt Williamson may be an Obrenovic by
birth, but his claim to be the head of the family would have to be settled by
genealogists as well as a College of Heraldry. I myself am a Robinson, but I
greatly doubt that I am closely related to the originator of the line,•- one
Lewilyn ap Tango of Wales •

                                WHAT JIM MOSELEY IS REALLY LIKE
                                - - by Sandy M-;;'seley - -

                People have often asked me what it's like being the wife of a UFO
researcher. Life has been an adventure since the day I married Jim. We were on
our way to be married when Jim received a collect call from California. One of
his fans had heard disturbing "rumors," and she wanted to make sure he didn't
marry anyone she didn't personally approve of. With her half-hearted approval
we eloped, and lived haphazardly ever after.
                One of our more unique wedding gifts was a Voodoo doll which Rich­
ard Ogden later explained was sent to us "to curse our unborn child."
                Many mornings we have been awoken by the Mystic Barber. Morning
calls are a mortal sin in our nocturnal lives. Recently we were awakened by a
mysterious telephone voice named "Alexander," who invited us for a steak din­
ner in New York City. Mr. Alexander, who refused to give his first name, hint­
ed all evening that he was a man from Outer Space. Said hes "People from other
planets smoke too much because of the air here•••••I smoke two packs of ciga­
rettes a day. But I don 1 t drink." The following evening he was caught nurtur­
ing a straight Scotch. The next day he disappeared in the cloak of mystery he
had arrived in!
                Jim receives letters and phone calls daily, accusing him of being
a member of the CIA, FBI, Air Force, Silence Group, International Bankers, the
Zionist Movement, the Communist Party, and the John Birch Society. In reality,
we belong to no social, religious or political organizations, except the Sau­
cer and Unexplained Celestial Events Research Society. This consists of sever­
al dozen members who meet on the third Friday of every month to discuss cur­
rent UFO events. These meetings are very interesting and also very funny at
times. Recently, an inventor was discussing his invention of a flying saucer
operated by free energy, when a woman in the audience jumped up and began
shouting, "My flying saucer is better than your flying saucer!"
                My first experience as a hostess was at a flying saucer party. The
                                                                      guests included Mr. and Mrs. Al
                                                                      Bender, Dr. and Mrs. Leon David­
                                                                      son, Gray Barker, Dominick Luc­
                                                                      chesi, Augie Roberts, Jack and
                                                                      Mary Robinson and Hans Santessen.
                                                                                Up until the party, Dr.
                                                                      Davidson (or "Dr. D," as he is
                                                                      sometimes called), did not be­
                                                                      lieve that Al Bender existed. So,
                                                                      before taking Al's picture with a
                                                                      polaroid camera, he took a "con­
                                                                      trol" shot of someone else. The
                                                                      "control" came out very clearly.
                                                                      But imagine the expression on Dr.
                                                                      D 1 s face when Al's picture failed
                                                                      to develop,and a pale blur emerg­
                                                                      ed! Mrs. D, who has no interest
                                                                      in saucers, sat quietly all eve­
                                                                      ning, and periodically apologized
                                                                      for her husband 1 s interest and
                                                                      enthusiasm.
                                                                               Mrs. Bender was lively and
                                                                      charming, but re.fused to discuss
                                                                      saucers or Al 1 s book. Al was ner­
   "Personally, I don't think they'll ever set it off tl,e l!l'ountf'
                                                                      vous, but bravely accepted the
                                                                      teasing he received.
12.

            The most impressive guest was Gray Barker. Although I had been in­
doctrinated against him, I liked him immediately, - something that has caused
confiict in our household. I just can't help liking him. He is witty, kind,
and a litUe shy. The party was a great success, and even Mrs. D appeared to
have enjoyed herself.
            What is Jim Moseley REALLY like? He has eyes "as blue as the bot­
tom of Loch Ness," dedicates most of his time to saucer research, and is very
witty. I am twelve years his junior, and he once remarked, "Darling, do you
know why we get along so well? It's because I read your child psychology book�
He is very adventurous, loves jungles, mountain climbing, exploring, and has
periodic wanderlust. He intended to explore the New Mexico desert and caves
looking for Deros recenUy. I'll admit I was worried because of the dangers
involved; but because I became very ill and plans were complicated at the last
minute, he was unable to go.
            Although he loves animals (we have a cat named Arnold that climbs
drapes and loves water) - he is a bullfight fan. He loves photography, and
collects old victrola records and pre-Columbian antiques. He is interested in
ESP, spiritualism, psychology (I'm responsible for that!), the legitimate
theatre, and "sick" paintings. His favorite painting is one in our living room
called "The Road to Oblivion."
            He has vices too - crushes at least two packs of cigarettes a day
into his den ashtray, drinks and swears moderately, and watches television as
he fills his more than two dozen scrapbooks of travel photos from around the
world.
            Jim is a sentimentalist, but likes to think of himself as a skeP­
tic. He saves all his correspondence, keeps a diary, buys me no-occasion
gifts (but dispises holidays and birthdays), and gets emotional at parades. He
is attracted to riots and other outdoor gatherings.
            We agree on almost everything, except his taste in art, which I
find ludicrous. We live by no schedule, and are considered non-conformists,
although Jim doesn't feel comfortable without a suit and tie.
            He is tall and thin, while I'm short and presenUy round, as we're
expecting a litUe Saucerite in September, whom we secreUy hope will� fol­
low in his father's footsteps. Jim's nickname is "Huesito" - Spanish for "lit­
Ue bones." Mine is "Silhouette," because of my present figure.
            Next to the United States, Jim's favorite place is Lima, Peru. He
hates cold weather, feels at home in the jungle, and speaks nuent Spanish
with a horrible accent.
            His favorite way to spend an evening alone is to roam around Man­
hattan, listening to extremist speeches and sidewalk quarrels. He can spend
hours listening to old-time music played on a piano. Although he can't carry a
tune, he enjoys playing the drums at a certain East Side cabaret (if I'm not
around.) - We don't like formality, do exacUy as we please, and really enjoy
life. So, being the wife of this particular fiying sau-
cer researcher is wonderful l                                       ..__ __,:91MA�]

                             RECENT NEWS STORIES
             SAUCER NEWS EDITOR UNABLE TO GO ON CAVE EXPEDITION: In our June
issue we announced your F.ditor 1 s intention to join an expedition to a myster­
ious cave in New Mexico. The trip was scheduled for early June, and the leader
of the group was a student of the occult named R. L. Dobbins, a resident of
Magdalena, New Mexico.
             One of our first.communications from Mr. Dobbins read in part as
follows, 11 There is a strange cave somewhere near this locale. Two discs the
size of car headlights have been seen coming from it each night, and going up­
ward out of sight. Two unusual small doors have also been seen, back a few
feet, in the mouth of the cave. At the cave entrance, there is a hole about 20
feet in diameter and 200 feet deep. When the members of the last expedition
descended to a depth of 20 feet, there was such an effect upon the spine that
no one was able to go down any further. Each individual's hair stood straight
up and stayed that way until out of the cave. There is something down there in
the way of life. I have never seen a Daro, but I would not be shocked if I
were to see one•••• n
             Our interest was aroused, and we entered into regular communica­
tion with Mr. Dobbins. He explained that a five-day hike was anticipated, and
he assured us that we would be allowed to take photographs of whatever was
seen, and to write the story fully for SAUCER NE.WS afterwards.
             We fully intended to go through with the adventure, but a series
of rather unusual events began to unfold. Of a half-dozen other expedition
members in the eastern United States, it developed that all had to cancel out
because of bad health or other reasons. Then, Sandy (your Editor's wife) be­
came quite seriously ill, and ran a very �gh fever for several days. The ill­
ness, originally diagnosed as nu, failed to respond to antibiotics, and she
was finally removed to a hospital, where she gradually recovered. This, plus a
number of other occurrences which were probably unrelated to Sandy's illness
or to the expedition, finally convinced us that we would be unwise to under­
take such a dangerous trip. We therefore reluctantly wrote to Mr. Dobbins,
canceling out.
             Our latest letter from Dobbins, reporting on the results of the
expedition, reads in part as follows, "The expedition was to extend across a
desolate region and search all caves on a pre-planned route to the cave of ma­
jor interest•••••Several days before the members were to arrive in Magdalena,
the City Marshall took pursuit to a brilliant reddish round light which was
seen on a nearby hillside. Just as he and his men would get near the object,
it would move to another hill within seconds. The object was pursued for three
consecutive nights•••••On the day of the arrival of the members, our telephone
went out of order, ,!:h2 members � � (italics ours), and something went
wrong with every mechanical mechanism connected witb the exploration. It would
take several pages to tell you about how many things went haywire, all within
a few hours. It was as though some unseen power had everything under its con­
trol. The expedition was finally attempted a week later. Nothing in the way of
a force field or a disc at the cave was observed. It was a great physical­
hardship getting to the cave. I do not think the journey was necessary, be­
cause whatever the force was, that unseen force came to� before we ever got
started!"
            SAUCER LANDING IN MONTANA& A report from Lewistown, Montana, dated
March 9th, tells us of a strange sighting two weeks previously, made by a her­
mit living in a mountainous area near there. Amos Biggs, called "the hermit of
the Snowy Mountains," saw a strange contraption hover and then land on the ice
of Crystal Lake. He said that the object cast a brilliant, almost radiant
light as it hovered over the lake and finally dropped to the ice in the black
14.
stillness of the night. Biggs stated that the UFO appeared to be silver in
color, was saucer-shaped on the bottom, and had a sma.11 oval compartment at
the top. A door mysteriously appeared in the oval part of the saucer, but no­
body got out. The object remained on the lake for about 10 minutes. Then the
door closed and the saucer arose quickly from the lake and disappeared into
the east. Biggs said that he detected only a slight buzzing sound from the
object while it was in motion.

             MYSTERIOUS DISAPPEARANCE IN NEW JERSEY, We have a follow-up on the
item which appeared at the very bottom of Page 22 of our June issue, describ­
ing the disappearance of a small boy from his home in Vineland, Cinnaminson
Township, N. J. The date of the incident as given in the June issue was March
18th, but according to our latest information, it was December 17th, 1962. In
any case, the basic facts are the same: The little boy, named Billy, was left
without supervision for just a few minutes. When his mother returned to the
yard in which the child had been playing, he had mysteriously vanished. Over
.500 members of the police, National Guard, and Civil Defense, as well as vol­
unteers, spent two days carefully combing 2.5 square miles looking for the boy,
without result. The strangest thing of all is that a bloodhound, which picked
up the child's trail outside the house, came to a tree in the yard where Billy
had been playing, and stopped dead in his tracks. The trail apparently ended
right theret - In all the intervening months, the distraught mother has re­
ceived no ransom note and the police have received no clue.
           SAUCER BRIEFS: As mentioned on Page 11, in Sandy Moseley's article,
a middle-aged man with a strange foreign accent contacted your Editor last
April, and made every possible effort to convince us that he was from another
planet. He identified himself only as "Mr. Alexander." He went so far as to
promise us an all-expense-paid tour around the world, lecturing on saucers.
Then, a few days later, after having met a few other saucer researchers in the
New York area, Mr. Alexander dropped out of sight as mysteriously as he had
appeared. Recently he or a very similar-looking individual went to St. Louis
and "hushed up" a UFO enthusiast there. - We never did find out just what Al­
exander's "angle" was, but we did establish that he is no extraterrestrial ••••
A new organization called The British U.F.O. Association has been formed in
England. It includes "every known British society whose primary interest is
the direct investigation of Unidentified Flying Objects� Eleven such organiza­
tions are listed on the letterhead. Interested persons should write to G.N.P.
Stephenson, 12 Dorset Road, Cheam, Sutton, Surrey, Englando••••Desmond Leslie,
co-author with George Adamski of "Flying Saucers Have Landed," was in the Eng­
lish newspapers last May, but not because of anything to do with saucers. It
seems that Mr. Leslie stepped in front of the cameras during a television pro­
gram, and aimed punches at a critic who had given a bad review to a play in
which Leslie's wife was appearing. Leslie said later that his intention was
not to hurt the critic but to "make a protest against corrupt criticism� •••

           SIGHTSEEING SAUCER VISITS STATUE OF LIBERTY: Many of the East Coast
papers carried stories of a 11 meteor 11 which was seen traveling south on the
night of March 25th. It seems strange that no seismographic reports of shock
waves or any other reports of the meteor's impact were ever made. Even though
the meteor traveled from the coast of Maine to Norfolk, Va. at extremely low
altitudes, it evidently did not strike the earth. For those readers who find
the antics of such an unusual meteor strange or hard to believe, we give the
story of one of the witnesses, who was personally interviewed by SAUCER NE.WS
Assistant Editor John J. Robinson.
            On March 25th Dudley C. Troy, who lives in Staten Island, N.Y. and
works in Jersey City, N.J., was driving south on Halliday St. in Bayonne, N.J.
at 10:10 p.m., when he suddenly saw a large object come sloping down out of
the sky. The object was above Caven Point, which is on the Jersey shore of New
York Bay, directly opposite the Statue of Liberty. The descending UFO leveled
off and proceeded southward at an elevation Troy estimated to be about 200
feet, at a distance of nearly a half mile away. It seemed to be traveling
                                                                           17.
about 40 miles per hour. Troy stated that the object was so low that houses
sometimes obscured it from view as it passed behind them.
            He freely admitted that the UFO frightened him, even though he had
served in the seabees in Africa as well as on submarine patrol in Port Sco­
tia. Mr. Troy said that it was as big as a house and resembled a giant pin­
wheel which pulsated with a yellowish-red glow. Somehow the object also re­
minded him of a Staten Island ferry boat, for it seemed to have some sort of
lighted windows. The disc-like UFO soon turned its edge on him and receded
from view, presenting only a thin cigar-shaped shadow in the slightly overcast
starlit sky, for the night was a moonless one. - During his observation, Mr.
Troy was also aware of a peculiar swishing sound as the object slowly revolved
through the air.

            FORTEAN ITEMS: Our old friend the Loch Ness Monster was spotted
again last April 22nd by two ladies who were driving along the lake's eastern
shore. They stopped their car to watch the creature swimming along about 400
yards from shore, and they could see the Monster's three humps clearly•••••An­
other brief news item, dated June 14th, informs us that the Loch Ness Monster
has again been caught on movie film. We hope to receive further information
about this•••There seems to be a rash of strange animals in the eastern United
States. First, there's a creature in the Highland Park Zoo in Pittsburg which
appears to be a cross between a raccoon and a possum, though such is official­
ly considered to be impossible. The animal looks a lot like a raccoon, - ex­
cept that he's about half the size, is almost bald.-headed, has a bit sharper
nose, lacks some coon markings, and has a tail something like a possum's. The
animal was trapped several months ago after a local woman complained that
something was prowling around her back yard every night. - Then, in Huntington
Long Island, a pink squirrel was captured recently, and in Plainview, Long Is­
land, a green puppy was born into a normal litter in which all the other PUP­
pies were brown. The dog was named Pistachio. An expert examined Pistachio and
declared that although his hair is definitely greenish, the roots are white.
He predicted that the dog will turn a conventional white color eventually•
            ••••A ball of blue ice dropped on the town of Tonawanda, New York,
at noon on May 6th, and bounced off the garage of a man named Arthur Krolick.
A neighbor actually saw the ball fall. He looked up, but saw nothing overhead
except the blue sky, - more or less ruling out the possibility that the ice
fell from an aircraft••••• Two strange incidents involving fireballs occurred
within 24 hours of each other in Virginia around April 20th. The first was in
Lynchburg, where Mrs. Shirley Dunn reported seeing a fiery object, spherical
in shape and greenish in appearance. The ball of fire fell into a gravel
driveway belonging to a neighbor, who was awakened by the nash of light ac­
companying the fall. The driveway was later examined in daylight, and a small
scorched area about the size of a grapefruit was found. Mrs. Dunn said that
there was a small green stone of light weight at the center of the scorched
area. - In Appomattox, Virginia, a fireball was spotted by Harry w. Ranson and
his family, as well as by many other residents. Ranson described it as soun.d.­
ing "like a shotgun blast." It was traveling at a low level, and looked to hi.a
like the light from the kind of nares used on highways. A piece of the fire
or name dropped off toward the ground, out of sight behind trees, and an hour
later Ranson found a forest fire 2½ miles away. He turned in an alarm, but the
fire burned three or four acres before being put out. The flaming •
                                                                  object which
caused the fire was apparently never found••••
            On May 1st, a mysterious gas spread over 30 square miles of east­
ern Los Angeles, forcing the evacuation of more than 14,000 school children,
and defying all efforts of investigators to learn its source•••There is a Pen­
tacostal church in Nassau, in the Bahama Islands, where a face resembling the
traditional representation of Jesus Christ can be seen on the wall. Your Edi-

              NEWS BRIEFS, On February 16th an Australian dairy farmer named
Charlie Brew reported seeing a fiying saucer which sent his horse and a herd
of cattle into a panic. The object appeared at 7&00 a.m., as Mr. Brew and his
son were milking their cows. It descended at a low rate of speed to a height
of only 75 to 100 feet above the ground. It looked as if it were going to
land, and then suddenly shot off in a westerly direction, disappearing into a
cloud. The UFO was about 25 feet wide and looked like a thick disc. It was
battleship gray in color and appeared to have a band of glass or plastic
around its circumference. There were also a number of protrusions. The UFO re­
volved and made a whooshing sound, according to Mr. Brew•••••Here•s a similar
story, which we would give a lot more space to, except that the April 1st
dateline (April Fool's Day) tends to invalidate its A farmer milking his cows
in Chileno Valley, near Petaluma, California, saw a fiying saucer land and
stampede the cattle. A group of humanoids from the saucer got out and placed a
calf aboard their craft before taking off. According to the local sheriff,
there was physical evidence to confirm the story, but he also noted that the
farmer in question is well known for his practical jokes••••
              Mr. Barker has kindly passed along a clipping to us, dated May
21st, concerning a mysterious fiying object which chased a car along a lonely
road in South Australia. The car was driven by a 17-year-old youth, who asked
that his name not be released by the newspaper. He said that he was driving
along a country road at about 9&JO p.m., when he saw an object at the side of
the road. Then, when his car was 20 yards away from the object, a bright light
suddenly started to shine from it, dazzling him. As he pulled up, the UFO mov­
ed across the road to the other side. He then speeded up his car. The light
shot straight up into the air and appeared to follow the ear as he drove along
at between 50 and 60 miles per hour. The light was as bright as the arc light
used in electric welding. - A similar report was made a few days earlier in
another part of South Australia, where a carload of people claimed that a
bright light, which changed from red to white in color, followed their car for
several mlles••••A saucer movie, taken by a Wisconsin girl in 1960, was appar­
ently lost by the Air Force en route to Wright-Patterson Field. The film. was
sul::mitted by a newspaper to Traux Air Force Base in Madison, Wisconsin. This
base admitted having received the film and having forwarded it to Wright-Pat­
terson, but the latter denied that it ever arrived there••••
                Four college students saw a group of bright luminous circular ob­
  jects, traveling at tremendous speeds over a drive-in theatre near Ripon, Wis­
  consin, last April 19th. Some of the UF0 1 s were silver in color, and others
  were a bright orange. They made several maneuvers, including a 90-degree turn
  which would have been impossible for normal aircraft. A detective sent to the
  theatre to investigate the incident also saw the saucers himself, according to
  the students•••••The mystery of the origin of the 11 fireflies 11 seen in orbit by
  John Cil.enn and other astronauts has apparently been solved. It seems that the
  "fireflies" come from the thrusters of the altitude-control system•••••On June
  10th, a strange triangular-shaped piece of metal fell on a farm belonging to
  Miguel Perez, near Concordia, Argentina. We received this report from our new
  (and exclusive) South .American press clipping service. According to the story,
  the metal is "obviously the product of an advanced technology, 11 and has on its
  surface "various inscriptions with unknown markings." Attempts to analyze the
  metal have failed. The material is extremely light and resistant, and is of a
  blue color, suggesting that it experienced very high temperatures before fall­
  ing to the ground••••
                A clipping from Hobart, Tasmania, dated February 5th, 1963, tells
  of a saucer landing which occurred the previous October 28th. The landing oc­
  curred on the road between Salisbury and Elizabeth, and was witnessed by a
  high school teacher named Mrs. E. D. Silvester, who stated that she and her
  three children watched the object for about 40 minutes before it took off.
  They also saw a hwuanoid outside the UFO, wearing a helmet of some sort, with
  a "gas mask" attached to it. No further details are given ••••• A planet outside
  the Earth's solar system and never seen by human eyes has been discovered by
  means of painstaking scientific calculations. The star and its planet are six
  light years away from Earth. The planet is about 500 times as large as Earth,
  and is thought to have too low a temperature to support life in an.y form ••••
  Finally, we learn that flying saucers have now become respectable enough to be
  included in the Encyclopedia Britannica. For the first time, the 1963 edition
  of Britannica has an article on saucers. It is by Professor Joseph Hynek,
  Chairman of the Astronomy Department of Northwestern University, and long-time
  Air Force consultant on the subject. Hynek claims that most UFO cases have
  been solved but concludes by sayings "Nevertheless, it must be recognized that
  knowledge of the universe and of the physics of our atmosphere is still imper­
  fect. UFO sightings, as long as they continue, will merit serious study and
  may lead to advances not only in physical knowledge but in the area of human
  behavior as well."
