Neanderthal Man. "Reasoned speculation" is most assuredly not something that
the authors of "The Shadow of the Unknown" have invented, rediscovered, or
stumbled upon.
              In any case, if one could assume that this method was put into
practice, then a very interesting and informative book could be expected,

glimmer of hope gave way once again to disillusionment upon discovering that

              SPACE   SHIPS
            OVER TIMES SQUARE
                              • by Ed Sparks•

(EQ.i,j,gr 1 �: The above is the pseudonym of an engineer whom we have met,
        s

and who does not want to use his real name for fear o! ridicule. The story he
tells is a fantastic one, but we feel that he is sincere. Together with his
manuscript, Mr. Sparks submitted a map of Manhattan showing the pattern of
saucer nights, and a photosta.t of a sto:ry fi-om the New York Daily News of
Monday, July 19th, 1937, which ties in with the stoey, as you will see below.
Lack of space prevents us from publishing the map Qr the photostat, but both
are in our files and will be shovm to any researcher who is interested.)

               At times one has to sit back and wonder why all stories concerning
the sighting of unidentified flying objects come from remote places such as
the deserts of California or Arizona. Why have we not heard of sightings in
heav:Uy-populated areas such as Chicago or New York City?
               As a matter of fact, I IIJYSelf made a sighting of many UFO's over
New York on the memorable afternoon of Sunday, JlU.y 18th, 1937; and except for
telling a few close friends, I have remained silent till now.
               It was a few minutes after 3 p.m. �men I decided to go to the mov­
ies in Times Square. The day was overcast, with clouds at about 4,000 feet. I
was walking north on the west side of 8th Avenue, between 38th and 39th
Streets, when ury eye was caught by an intense beam of light, similar in color
to th.at en,J.tted by the coils of an electric range, The light seemed for an in­
stant to bQ bel,lning from a renector onto IIIY face. I looked up and saw it was
being emitted by dual headlights of what at first appeared to be a low-nying
plane. The lights seemed to be going on and off at regular inte?'vals, and
their origin was at a spot in the sky above Columbus Circle, at an angle of
thirty degrees from the northern horizon. The speed of the approaching craft
was slow, i.e., between thirty and fifty miles per hour. No sound whatsoever
could be heard, and I could see that it had no propeller in front. Suddenly it
made a maneuver which was entirely unconventional for airplanes of that day -
or for planes of the present day, for that matter.
               This maneuver was a sharp thirty degree turn to the east. I gazed
in amazement as I noticed that the wings did not taper back to the center of
the body of the air frame. Instead, they tapered in circular form back to the
tail I At the tail section, the craft did not have any elevators. Also, the
rudder was of peculiar design. It appeared webbed as though it could be fold­
ed and raised or lowered into the body of the craft.
               Suddenly the UFO disappeared from view behind tall buildings; but
seconds later, another craft with dual red beams could be seen coming out of a
cloud bank. It followed the path of the first craft, making the same maneu­
vers. As I searched the sky for more of the m,ystery ships, I spotted in the
distance, north by northeast of me, an entire neet of them! The formation was
different from any I had ever seen. Among the craft was a huge pantella-shaped
dirigible, which did not have the usual gondola on its underbelly. There was a
row of square windows from the front to the rear. Dirigibles are supposed to
have motors, but none were visible, and there was no noise! Not a sound was
being emitted from any of these ships, and they cast no shadows, due to the
overcast skies. The night speed was slow, but soon the neat was lost from
sight behind buildings, just as the first UFO had disappeared from view.
               I again searched the skies and saw another huge dirigible-shap�d
l.2.
object at 85 degrees above the horizon, almost directly overhead. It was mov­
ing from west to east, and must have been directly over 42nd Street, or be­
tween 41st and 42nd. I glanced around tor see if other people were observing
the amazing phenomena I was seeing, but traffic was moving normally, and there
were'few pedestrians on that block. I kept asking mysel.f why other people were
not looking up. This huge craft was moving in the direction of Long Island,
and it too had a l.ong row of square windows, except for the last window in the
row, which was circular and located slightly higher than the others.
             Then· I saw a symb9l of identification on the craft. The symbol was
a crescent, and ·I wondered if this meant that the mystery ships were from some
Mid-Eastern country. Then the real.ization struck me that these must be craft
from. another planet! Perhaps they had come to salute the city of New York be­
fore making a landing. They all seemed to be going in the direction of Long
Island, and I asked myself if they intended to use some unpopulated area out
there as a landing field.
             I looked to the south and saw the front end of still another for­
mation of ships going parallel with approximately 30th Street, at about sixty
degrees from the southern horizon. This formation, too,· was traveling east. I
saw tremendous flashes of light at mid-ship of a huge dirigible-type craft.
These l.ight flashes made the interior visible, and in the mid-section of the
ship I could see what appeared to be- a blank wall. of a dark gray color. The
flashes occurred intermittently as the UFO traveled its course. Before the
tail end of this craft was obscured by buildings, another identical one ap­
peared, 85 degrees above the southern horizon. This one was traveling diago­
nall.y across Manhattan, from the southwest to the northeast. It was the last
of the huge pantella type craft to be spotted that day. Through its windows
fla�hes of bright light, like lightning, were being emitted - reminiscent of
the intermittent flashing of a welder's torch. This ship too had windows al.ong
its side from front to rear, in the same locations as those on the other
cigar-shaped craft, but there was no symbol of any kind.
             As the tail end of this last craft was obscured by tall buildings,
I ran to the corner of 39th Street to again see its center section with the
weird inner flashing of light. I estimated the size of this ship to be one
mile in length! Suddenly the frequency of the internal sparking lights in­
creased, and an aurora of darlmess surrounded the craft. A roar went up from
the area of Times Square, such as one would hear at a ball game. Through my
mind nashed the thought that finally the many other people in the area had
seen what was going on. I turned to two people near me and yelled, "Look,
space shipst" One man looked up just in time to see a smaller craft, different
in design from the others, which was traveling rather swiftly from. north to
south parallel to Eighth Avenue. The fell.ow was visibl.e disturbed, but the
other man did not look up, and therefore saw nothing.
             I wondered if I should go to Ti.mes Square and speak to other wit­
nesses, or if I should go home and listen to the radio and obtain complete de­
tails from the radio broadcasts. I decided on the latter course, and went home
expecting to hear about landings on Long• Island; but no such• report came in,
and the next day's newspapers made no mention of the fiight, which included a
total of at least J6 craft in all.I The only vaguely confirmatory evidence was
a story in the New York News headlined "Quake Rocks Long Isl.and, n which de­
scribed a three minute series of tremors felt on the Island; but in m::, opin­
ion, it was AS2ii an earthquake. At a later date I learned from a friend that
there was indeed scae commotion in Times Square that afternoon, at the time I
heard the roar of voices. It seems that for about thirty seconds it got
strangely dark for that time of day. The explanation given was that somebody
must have pulled the main power switch at the Consolidated Edison Company.
Thus, through rationalization and ignorance, one of the greatest events of our
times went unrecorded. After all these years, I still. have no explanation!

Archaeologists state that these stone forts were erected around a framework of
logs. They say that the forts were captured and set on fire, whereupon the
logs burned and left flue-like chann�ls through which flames roared, creating
the intense heat which melted the upper stone ramparts and left them vitrified.

burned so hot that it melted the rim of the chimney, regardless of the

 to a glass-like vitrification. That the forts may have been attacked by a

 dio-active force - seems to me to be the best guess and the most logical of

 in the holocaust. To me, however, 11 wyld fire" from the UFO's supplies a more

means "experilllental, 11 and 4 means it is the fourth craft I've worked on. Elec­
tro-Craft means it is electric, but of course this is a general term and does
not say very much, other than the fact that it operates on what we call elec­
tricity. The big difference between the X-4 Electro-Craft and any other elec­
trical device is the unique means in which it obtains electrons, and what it
does with them in order to change energy to work power and allow the craft to
rise vertically, silently, and then take off in a horizontal direction at
great speed!
             The one I am building now is approximately 18 feet in diameter,
but it might be larger by the time we are finished. The reason for this is
predicated upon the weight on the inner section, which is increasing. This
might necessitate a change in the outer diameter to acoomodate a larger sur­
face area, giving the craft more thrust power. In other words, in the case of
18.
an airplane (and the laws of aerodynamics) a large airplane will not get
off the ground with small wings. In the case of the Electro-Craft,        however,
there is no connection with aerodynamics as far as I can see, because it uses
electrical pressures, and/or electrical 11 voids 11 or vacuums. Thus it could pos•
sibly fly at much higher altitudes, not needing an environment of air or at­
mosphere, as airplanes must have.
            In the case of the famous U-2, which flies at very high altitudes
where the air is thin, secret additions were incorporated into the plane's
design so that it could do its job quite well. It has a much larger wing area,
plus jet assists at strategic locations on its fuselage, etc., so that it
could hover at high altitudes for photography, etc. In my opinion the Electro­
Craft will be far superior, not only because it could hover at much greater
altitudes, but also because it will be capable of much higher speeds in a hor­
izontal or a vertical direction. Also, it could be adapted for flights into
outer space in the future.
           But I want to get back to my idea on how to raise funds to complete
the X-4 Electro-Craft and sponsor a convention on the East Coast: A few weeks
ago, the money alloted for the completion of the craft ran out, and I am back
to outdoor advertising, designing, building, painting, lettering, etc., to
support my family and meet my financial obligations. Thus, work on the craft
has been limited to only a few hours each week, and at this rate it will not
be ready for testing before 1965; so here is my suggested solution:
            I can supply a complete set of plans for a four-foot replica of
the larger 18 foot model, which can do almost everything its big brother will
do, but on a much smaller scale. It would be a simplified version, without the
thrust or speed of the big brother, nor would it cost anywhere near as much to
build. It would operate by radio remote control on the ground by the man who
builds it. At night it glows and pulsates in various colors, mostly blue. In
the daytime its shiny surface reflects the sunlight and looks just like a sau­
cer from outer space as it dips and turns. This is the same craft (the X-3)
which I flew successfully back in the summer of 1961.
            I would supply a set of plans for this 4-foot replica of the Elec­
tro-Craft, together with complete instructions and step-by-step drawings, as
well as a list of parts and where to buy them. These plans would be sent
through the mail in a mailing tube upon receipt of $25.00 check or money order.
            The first three men to complete and fly the X-4 would each be
awarded a one hundred dollar U. S. Government savings bond, and an offer to
work with me (on salary) on the larger 18-foot model, which will be capable of
carrying a man.
            This 18-foot craft, when completed, will be test flown by me for
sane 6 or 7 interested government and civilian officials. They are only inter­
ested in a working model, and you can 1 t blame them. So this is an opportunity
not only for me, but for the men who have the talent, etc., to complete their
4-foot model and fly it. This would be a great chance to form a company and
manufacture the X-4 on a government or civilian contract. The money received
from selling the smaller models would be used to complete the 18-foot Electro­
Craft and also to help sponsor the above-mentioned space convention, with
radio, television, and other advertising media. It would also help to pay the
salaries of the men working with me.
            Without a doubt, many people will complete their models and fly
them at the convention grounds. This of course will help to sell many more. I
can see a lot of good coming from all this. It would probably be the biggest
saucer convention ever held in the United States! The first East Coast space
convention, which I held on my farm back in 1958, was the largest ever held in
the East, and we did not have a radio-controlled flying saucer to show the
public - much less one that will carry a man! I also have photos of my earlier
flying models of the Electro-Craft, and I will send them to SAUCER NEWS soon.

            Fil:ECTRCMAGNETIC SAUCER .ti NEVADAr A sighting which gives further
confirmation to John J. Robinson's article in this issue of SAUCER NEWS oc­
curred on November 14th in Carson City, Nevada. A resident of Carson City
named Blanche Pritchett reported early that morning that she saw a disc-shaped
object, "tremendous in size," hovering in the southwestern skies. The lady al­
so said that a brilliant shaft of light from the UFO illuminated a nearby hill.
            The huge object stood stationary in space while she watched it
from a window of her hane. It had "an odd green color with a golden cast," and
Mrs. Pritchett also noted that a "vibratory hum" came from the object.
            She first noticed the UFO at about 4:45 a.m. after seeing the
drapes of her bedroom lit up from a glow outside. She added that a radio sta­
tion which she was listening to at the time �ill      �m       while the object
was lighting the skies. It came back on again after the UFO disappeared!
            Other persons phoned police, the weatherman, -and a radio station
in nearby Reno and reported the same or a similar object in the sky southwest
of Reno. No explanation for the phenomenon could be found.

            RECENT LANDING lli ARGENTINA, Fraa our special correspondent in Ar­
gentina, Henning Von Rippel, we learn that landings of flying saucers are
still occuring !'requently in that country. (See the December 1962 and March
1963 issues of SAUCER NEWS for earlier landing reports.)
            The latest case involves truck driver Eugenio Douglas, who, on
October 19th, told police the following story, which we have translated !'rom
Spanish:
            "I was driving my truck to Isla Verde when a luminous object - ap­
parently a spaceship - surrounded by a light of blinding brilliance, came down
near me. Three very tall beings, dressed in strange clothes and carrying sc:ae
unknown kind of weapons, came down out of it toward me. I lost control of my
vehicle and drove into a ditch, but I finally got back onto the road and kept
on driving at high speed. The three beings kept coming after me. A blinding
red light came from their craft. The creatures followed me for about a mile
and a half, and shot out a gas which burned and caused me to lose my vision
temporarily. When I finally arrived in the outskirts of the town of Monte
Maiz, they suddenly stopped the persecution. They then got back into their
craft, which disappeared into the distance."
            The article goes on to state that Senor Douglas had indeed been
burned by something, and that the doctors who examined him were not able to
find the cause of the burns!
            Another sighting, from the Buenos Aires newspaper "El Mundo," dat­
ed November 16th, translates in part as follows:
            "Mar del Plata: Upon hearing the words • fiying saucer, • hundreds
of residents of this city rushed to their balconies or the nearest open space,
early yesterday morning, to see a cigar-shaped object which was giving out an
intermittent light, which at times was white and at times bluish. This object
hovered over the city for 15 minutes, from 12:JO to 12:45 a.m. It finally
disappeared at high speed toward the north. The machine - for, due to the way
it maneuvered, it could not have been a natural phenomenon - was over the
western part of the city, about thirty degrees above the horizon. Among the
witnesses was Horacio Tarifeno, chief of the information service of radio sta­
tion LO9, who went out on his terrace after receiving many excited telephone
calls about the object."

            CLOSE SIGHTING IR rnQ: At least four people in Meridian, Idaho,
saw a UFO at very close range on the evening of October 24th. The witnesses
20.

included Mrs. Albert Gordon and her two sons, one of whom made the following
report:
            "My brother Russell heard a noise over the house at 8:45 p.m. It
faded away, but then came closer again and hovered over the house. My mother,
Russell, and I looked outside. It was dark out, but as nearly as we could see,
the object was perfectly round and about 35 feet in diameter. A red light on
its side flashed on once and then went off quickly. It made a very deep hum­
ming noise. It just hung about eight feet above our willow tree, very low and
motionless, and then slowly moved on after about five minutes. We were all
pretty scared."
            The family, as well as the F.A.A., discounted the possibility that
the craft could have been a helicopter.

            FORTEAN �'
            Several unusual disturbances on the Moon have been reported re-
cently, including one on December 4th, spotted by Air Force lunar mappers at
Lowell Observatory. This time, motion pictures were taken of the ruby-red
eruption, which is considered to be possibly volcanic in nature. It lasted for
an hour and a half.
            On Mars, a series of peculiar observations in October led to spec­
ulation that atomic explosions might be taking place! Bright flashes were ob­
served, lasting about five minutes each, and followed by mushroom-shaped
clouds characteristic of atomic bomb blasts.
            An item dated November 13th tells of the Mystery of the Disappear­
ing Lawn. It seems that New Jersey State Trooper Albert W. Waldron looked out
the window of his home in Ironia, N.J., one morning, and found a.hole ten feet
wide and fifty feet deep where the side lawn had been. He had it filled with
27 truckloads of dirt, but the next time there was a heavy rainstonn, the hole
reappeared. Apparently the cause has something to do with abandoned mines
nearby.
            On October 2nd, a mass migration of thousands of small frogs caus­
ed the temporary closing of Highway 24 near the California canmunity of Riego.
            A Jamaica, Long Island woman, driving a car blindfolded in a test
ride in Canada last swmner, made only one mistake during her demonstration of
alleged X-Ray vision, She hit a pedestrian. Her victim was not injured, but
she was hauled up on careless driving charges nevertheless.
            On November 12th, a 25-foot-long monster described as having a
flat head and a big mouth was reported by several people in a small town sixty
miles south of Johannesburg, South Africa.

             Last October, a priest was called in to exorcise a "water spirit"
from a country house in Farm.borough, Somerset, England. Mrs. Heidi Whittock­
Knott, owner· of the haunted house, finds that every time she shuts the front
door to go out, water begins pouring through the ceilings and running down the
walls. Water splashes from the sink and buckets fall over, even though there
is no one at home. Local plumbers and other workmen have been unable to find
an explanation, nor have parapsychologists who have visited the house. The
priest has asked to remain anonymous.
             The New York News of December 8th tells the story of a woman in
flint, Michigan, who can distinguish colors using only her hands while blind­
folded. The woman has norm.al vision, but a series of experiments are being
carried out on her in an attempt to gain knowledge that will aid the blind. It
is also said that there is a young woman in Russia who not only can identify
colors with her fingers, but can also read with them!
            In October, 39 whales allowed themselves to be washed up on a beach
at Wreck Bay, Australia, and died there. An official from a local university
explained that "mass suicides" of this breed of whales occur every two or
three years. The cause is a complete mystery to scientists.
             A weird story from April (1963) has only recently come to our at­
tention. It concerns the three-foot-long body of a creature that looks like a
devil. The body, entombed in a glass case, was accidentally dug up by a boy in
 Charlotte, North Carolina, and after newspaper publicity, more than 5,000 lo­
cal people came to see the strange creature. This "devil," or whatever it is,
 has claw-like nails on its hands, two tusk-like teeth, cloven hooves instead
 of feet, and horns protruding from its forehead. It is thought to have been a
 circus exhibit many years ago, but there is a dispute over whether the thing
was once alive, or whether it is a fake. Several museum authorities have shown
 interest in it.
             Another case of ice falling from the sky occurred on October 13th,
 on a farm in upper New York State owned by a man named Hugo Wileen. A large
 barn on his property was utterly demolished by huge hunks of ice which hit
 with a great deal of noise. They apparently fell at very high speed. There
 were no airplanes overhead at the time, and the amount of ice was enough to
 fill several freight cars! It appeared to be natural ice rather than arti­
 ficial.
              The death of President John F. Kennedy was accurately predicted by
 Mrs. Jeanne Dixon, who has made many other uncanny predictions concerning fa­
 mous people. Among other things, she predicted Roosevelt's death, one ot
 Eisenhower's illnesses, Churchill's defeat as Prime Minister and his subse­
 quent "comeback," and the death of actress Carole Lombard.
              As discussed several times recently on the Long John Show, it is
 now believed that humans can be preserved at the time of death by deep freez­
 ing, and revived at a later time to liTe again. The cost would be about $8,800
 per •body.
            m     SAUCER SIGHTING lli MICHIGAN I Two deputy sheriffs in Port Hu­
ron, Michigan, had an unusual encounter with a UFO at about 5 a.m. last Novem­
ber 12th. They observed what was at first only
                                            •      a "nickering light" hovering
in the sky. As it got closer, it seemed at times that there was a big divided
window in the midst of the flashing light, but the officers could not be sure.
The UFO eventually approached to a height o.f only a .few hundred feet above the
ground, a short distance from their patrol car. The deputies attempted to fol­
low it, reaching speeds up to 75 miles per hour in the chase, but the object
out-distanced them. They lost sight of it completely for awhile. It then reap­
peared, and the officers stopped the car, turned out their headlights, and
22.
shined spotlights onto the object. They then turned on the flashing light on
top of the patrol car, and in a few moments the UFO not only had a flashing
white light but it also seemed to be flashing a red light back at them, as if
in an attempt at communication! The UFO appeared to again be standing still in
the sky at that time, and they could hear a low sound of motors. Suddenly the
motors grew louder, and the object disappeared rapidly out of sight.

             Last October, Dr. Frank E. Stranges, author of "F1.ying Saucerama"
and other books·, was convicted in California of operating a "mail order diplo­
ma nu.U," in that he was issuing doctol' of divinity degrees for $25 each. A
recent form letter sent out by Stranges to his fans does not deny that he was
issuing such diplomas, but contends that this activity was legal. In any case,
by his own adluission, Stranges was fined $200 and made to spend the night in
jail. He now asks for $2,000 in donations to cover "court penalties, fines,
and attorney's fees."••••
            ;On Page 18 of our September issue, we printed a photograph taken
by your Edik.or in a Pentacostal church in Nassau, in the Bahama Islands. On
the back wall of this church, near the altar, all visitors (including your Ed­
itor) can see a face reselllbling the traditional representation of Jesus
Christ; but all attempts to photograph the face have supposedly failed. For
most readers of SAUCER NEWS, there was no face in the picture, but a few peo­

             We were surprised to learn recently of an incident that occurred
in July of 1947, just about a month after nying saucers started making na­
tional headlines. Eight five-foot balloons were sent up from the New York
Daily News building, in an effort to prove that people will report anything as
a flying saucer. Of the eight, one landed in the yard of Gladys Fusaro in
Huntington, Long Island, more than thirty miles away. It just happens that
Mrs. Fusaro has been interested in UF0 1 s and Fortean events as far back as
1942, and she is now a member of the SAUCER NEWS Staff. Out of some twenty
million people living in the New York metropolitan area, isn't it a strange
co-incidence that one out of only eight balloons sent up to ridicule flying
saucers should land in the yard of a UFO enthusiast? Or 1§. it a co-incidence
at alll?
             An in.formant who attended a slide lecture by contactee Buck Nelson
in St. Louis, Missouri, reports that a blurred picture of a human-like space­
man looked identical to a clearly-focused picture of Buck Nelson himself; and
a photo of the famous 300-pound space dog that sometimes visits him, was iden­
tical to that of a small lap dog shown in another picture! The lap dog was be­
ing held in the arms of a miscellaneous visitor at one of Buck's conventions
in Mountain View, Missouri.
             According to a recent issue of his bulletin, Jim Wales (a co-work­

 fered Mr. Wales money to take Laura Mundo 1 s book 11 F1.ying Saucers and the Fa­
 ther's Plan" off the market. Mr. Wales believes that the calls were either
from the "Silence Group" or from space visitors••••

            In the course of 93 shoddily-mimeographed pages, the staff of the
NJAAP carefully and meticulously destroy every semblance of the air of scien­
tific study that the Introduction of their book endeavored to create. The tome
runs the gamut from dogmatic and unquestioning acceptance of Aime Michel's
"orthoteny" hypothesis - which in reality has only been proven to have occur­
red during the 1954 French saucer nap - to APRO's fallacious "physical evi­
dence" allegations.
            The book, however, is not without its good points. An informative,
though tediously written, account of the Charlton Crater Mystery, together
with some other worthwhile material, unfortunately do not make up for its nu­
merous shortcomings. If you are in search of a mature, sober, and impartial
study of the UFO enigma rather than an inadequate rehash of material that has
already appeared elsewhere, you may safely pass this one by.

2.

           -          STORY -----
                            REGARDING -
                                      OUR -
                                          FEUD -
                                               WITH -
                                                    GRAY BARKER
            As promised in the March issue, we are now revealing for the first
time the true reason behind SAUCER NEWS' long feud with Gray Barker - a sub­
ject that has been the cause of much speculation over the years.
            Your Editor first met Barker in.the Fall of 1953, shortly before
Barker began publication of his magazine, then called The Saucerian. In the
months that followed, the two became fairly close friends. They wrote each
other regularly, and got together in Clarksburg as often as possible to dis­
cuss UFO•s. Both of them took saucer research very seriously in those days,
and worked closely together on a certain theory they had jointly arrived at
concerning the origin of saucers.
            Then, in the summer of 1954, about the time that the first issue
of SAUCER NEWS (then called "Nexus") was published, your Editor visited
Clarksburg again - this time accompanied by a young lady whom he had serious
intentions of marrying. Suffice is to say that Barker's charming manner suc­
ceeded in alienating the affection of this girl, so that the affair between
her and your Editor came to an abrupt end, as did the friendship between him
and Barker. Although Barker's attentions to the girl on that memorable weekend
were obvious, he later claimed that his encouragement of her infatuation for
him was unintentional. Eventually the girl married someone else - a person not
acquainted with either of the two researchers; but it was several years before
your Editor could fully forgive Barker for the incident.
            As neither Barker nor Moseley wanted to publicly admit the real
cause of their feud, they proceeded to attack each other in their respective
magazines about other things not related to the incident that destroyed their
friendship. They perpetrated hoaxes on each other, and made bitter accusations
against each other by letter and in print. Finally, with your Editor's mar­
riage in 1962, and his buying out of the Saucerian in 1963 due to Barker's fi­
nancial difficulties, he became appeased, and decided to forget the past. In
recent months a superficial friendship has again developed between the two
men, and it is hoped by both parties that the last vestiges of the feud will
soon be gone.
            Actually, it must be admitted that Barker and Moseley were never
far apart in their personal beliefs regarding most aspects of saucer research,
and it would certainly have been better for the whole saucer field if both had
acted more maturely. Saucerdom has wasted too much time and effort on petty
bitterness such as this; and were it not for the fact that researchers in gen­
eral are so hopelessly divided for a variety of reasons, the solution to the
saucer mystery might have been discovered and proven by now!

briefly describes the contents of each.

                       !fil:! !Q!Y£ :yECTURE SERIES CONTINUES
            Since April, 1962, the Discussion and Lecture Committee of SAUCER
NEWS has been holding open meetings in New York City. Our program this winter
and spring has been an unusually interesting one, featuring a different "name"
personality in the flying saucer field at each meeting.
            On February 21st, our speaker was Ranjee Shahani, a Professor of
English at Farleigh Dickinson University in Teaneck, N.J. Mr. Shahani, who is
the author of several books of literary criticism, lectured on "Space Travel
in Ancient India." Most of his talk described the inner workings of the human
personality and soul, rather than the legends of ancient India, but he was
well received by an enthusiastic audience.
            Just before the Shahani lecture, we learned that contactee Major
Wayne Aho, founder of Washington Saucer Intelligence, would be in town the
following week for only a few days; so on the spur of the moment we arranged
for him to be our guest on February 28th. Major Aho spoke about the flying
saucer convention that is being planned on the East Coast for this summer, and
he also touched upon religion, saucer sightings, and various aspects of cosmic
philosophy. The photo below, taken by Eugene Steinberg, shows him at the SAU­
CER NEWS podium.
            On Friday, the 13th of March, Albert K. Bender gave a fascinating
lecture entitled "The World of Kazik," describing detailed information given
to him by his space contacts concerning life on their home planet (i.e., Ka­
zik.) Part of the talk covered material already printed in Bender's famous
book, "Flying Saucers and the Three Men," but most of his time was devoted to
new information, notably about his private life during the past few years. It
seems that his visitors have given him a strange power that he never asked
for, whereby, whenever he wishes evil toward anyone, something terrible actu­
ally happens to that person. Mr. Bender gave five or six examples of this -
any one of which might well involve co-incidence, but in their sum total, they
do indeed denote that something very strange is going on. The most frightening
story related by the speaker was an incident on a bus, in which another com­
muter annoyed him daily by smoking a cigar in a seat immediately in front of
him or behind him. After many days of this annoyance, Bender found himself
wishing that the man
would burn himself with
his cigar; and a short
time later, he was as­
tonished to learn that
the man had burned to
death at his home, as a
result of smoking in bed!

used an ingenious device
to show pictures to the
audience, including some

                            LETTERS .!Q � EDITOR

            I am in receipt of your letter in which you offered a great many
criticisms of Laura Mundo's "Flying Saucers and the Father's Plan," which evi­
dently you have only recently read.
            We have, in fact, received a great many other criticisms from
readers. Mostly they object to the author's great departure from orthodox be­
liefs. By publishing a book I do not necessarily endorse what is in it, for we
publish a number of books containing divergent-ideas.
            It may interest you to know that the author herself is dissatis­
fied with our publication of her book. She holds that by correcting certain
passages we have changed its meaning.
            In checking back over some of the copy, I find that some such
changes have indeed been made. I can honestly state, however, that I was un­
aware of them as I edited the copy. Part of the finished type for offset
printing was set up by myself, and part of it by an outside assistant who has
since moved away. I cannot speak for the outside assistant, but I can point
out some things which might be construed by an imaginative person as rather
unusual:
            Usually when I am setting type for offset I go rather slowly. Dur­
ing the work on Laura's book, however, it seemed at times that my fingers
would fly, and I would have four or five pages set up before I knew it. I
don't know quite how to explain this. At other times I would have great dif­
ficulty just keeping at the job. Although the finished copy was checked by my­
self and a very good editor, some very obvious mistakes and departures from
the original text completely slipped by us and were printed before we spotted
them. I have never had this happen on any other work I have been connected
with.
            For these reasons, and because of one or two other incidents which
may have been imaginary, and which I will tell to nobody, I am seriously con­
sidering removing this book from circulation after our advance sale orders are
filled....              GRAY BARKER, Clarksburg, W. Va.

(See our review of "Flying Saucers and the Father's Plan" on Page?. -Editor.)
             Many thanks for your kind remarks about my Mars lecture, in the
March issue of SAUCER NEWS.· As my training is not in the physical sciences, I
do not think my views should be taken as seriously as those of men who have
had a form.al education in these fields.
             For the record, however, I would like to say that I have no con­
nection with the "intelligence community." Specifically, I do not work for the
CIA, DIA, NSA, ONI, G-2, MI-6, OGPHEW, or any other similar organization.
             May I use your magazine to make a general request? I would very
much like to get in touch with the Martian resident on Earth (or any other in­
terplanetary or interstellar operative.) This, of course, is not an offer to
engage in anything unpatriotic, but is only a friendly invitation to discuss
matters of interest to us both. It is a perfectly serious invitation. Perhaps
the contact might be made by letter, using your post office box as the ad­
dress. In order to establish identity, would the resident please give me a
brief answer to this question: "What is the possible connection between PK and
the argument from design?" I apologize for asking for a test, but I'm certain
everyone will understand that it is desirable. The answer to the question, by
the way, is beyond the ken of almost all terrestrial minds, but it will be
easy for a member of a higher species.

                        JEROME CLARK, Canby, Minnesota
              Book                      Reviews
                           - by Richard E. Wallace -

(Reviewer's Note: During the period of time that has elapsed since we last ap­
peared in print, we have been literally deluged by an almost equal amount of
pro and con mail. - Those of you who have been quick to voice dissent and
skepticism as to the impartiality of our commentaries, are apparently unaware
of the basic fact that, whatever conclusions we might formulate in perusing
books submitted to us for review, there must inevitably be many among you who
would find points of contention therein. One would indeed have to be naive to
entertain even the slightest expectation of satisfying everyone. - We    would
like to offer our sincere thanks for the many complimentary letters we have
received. Every effort will be made to reply to each and every one of you per­
sonally.)
FLYING SAUCERS�� FATHER'S� - by Laura Mundo (Saucerian Books)
.!!!§FATHER'S�� FLYING SAUCERS - by Laura Mundo (The Planetary Center)
             Each of these volumes would be an enigma,� il, even when tackled
individually. Thus in discussing them together, some fascinating and indeed
piquant bits of info:nnation can be unearthed.
              Originally, Gray Barker's highly profitable publishing concern was
commissioned to propagate these urgent and significant disclosures Mrs. Mundo
deigned to impart to us lesser beings. Mr. Barker's natural inclination to de­
lete the more esoteric teachings of the author, in order to make the book more
commercially palatable, was understandable - though it was not met with any
semblance of appreciation on the part of Mrs. Mundo. Accordingly, she and her
colleagues chose to prepare their own mimeographed edition to be distributed
gratis in an unexpurgated form, without any omissions that would deprive us of
full comprehension (or incomprehension) of that which it was imperative to in­
form us of.
              If we can presume to have interpreted the views of Mrs. Mundo cor­
rectly, she is saying that we earthlings have,for various nefarious and insid­
ious activities, been demoted from our former highly advanced planes of exist­
ence upon other worlds, to reside within the hostile and primitive society
that prevails upon this planet, until such time as our physical and spiritual
develoµnent have attained a state of perfection, whereupon we shall be rein­
carnated upward to our prior level·of evolution. If, however, a degradation of
our physical and spiritual being should percha.nee occur, we shall be relegated
to the confines of worlds vastly inferior to this one.
              We are presented with the supposition that "Visitors" from planets
whose state of development transcends our own have arrived here to assist us
in once again attaining the physical and spiritual refinement that we have in­
explicably lost. They have come now because of imminent planetary changes, the
precise nature of which has eluded us even after reading both versions of Mrs.
Mundo 1 s book.
              In recounting her experiences with her other-worldly companions,
Mrs. Mundo details her encounters with various extraterrestrial entities with
whom we are quite familiar, including George Adamski 1 s infamous celestial co­
hort, Orthon, who is identified to be in actuality "Enoch." At this point, one
must speculate as to why the eminent "Professor" was permitted to remain igno­
rant of this amazing "truth."
              Mrs. Mundo 1 s books are apparently a potpourri of just about every­
thing she managed to amass, copy, or if all else failed her, invent. She
latches onto George Adamski 1 s cosmic philosophy, which, stripped of its inter-
a.
planetary connotations, is a simple and straightforward plea for the abandon­
ment of the heinous devices of war in favor of eternal and everlasting broth­
erhood among men. She then utterly demolishes this philosophy to further her
own implausible and irrational views.
             She unsuccessfully endeavors to assimilate various Biblical phe­
nomena with the study' of flying saucers, going. so far as to assert her contact
with the evil proprieter of Hades, Satan! It might tax her imagination severe­
ly to conjure up some rational explanation as to, first of all, why she was
permitted this dubious honor, and secondly, how she was capable of surviving
so as to relate such an adventure.
             Both Mrs. Mundo 1 s and Gray Barker's version of the book contain
nume.rous printing flaws, resulting, apparently, from carelessness on the part
of this obstreperous duo. Mr. Barker has presumably expended much effort to
revert Mrs. Mundo 1 s manuscript from incoherence to a readable form, but he
does not entirely succeed, though he is to be commended for a laudable effort.
The unfortunate fact is that the property with which he dealt contained no­
thing one could edit into publishable shape.
             It may almost be superfluous to add that Mrs. Mundo 1 s purportedly
"corrected" edition, to which she adds a conglomeration of material that is
inherently as valueless as the basic work itself, certainly goes a long way to
justify the reasons Mr. Barker had for his editing. Neither volume has any fi­
nancial, or for that matter, any tangible value for anyone desirous of secur­
ing them. The flying saucer field has never had it worse!

            It is obvious that, in recent years, Gray Barker has found it in­
creasingly difficult to secure intelligible - however ineptly conceived - man­
uscripts for publication. Thus has he confronted the necessity of groping at
the bottom of the barrel for the fruits of his quest, out of sheer despera­
tion. One would presume that, with the promulgation of Mrs. Mundo 1 s book, Mr.
Barker had sunk to the lowest level of mediocrity it was possible to attain.
It is indeed regrettable that this assumption has proved unwarranted.
            Despite the varied and patently clear shortcomings of Mrs. Mundo 1 s
book, she nevertheless professed to be a living, breathing person. But now Mr.
Barker presumes the public to be of sufficient credulity as to accept the un­
likely possibility of the contents of 11 The Flying Saucers" being the utter­
ances of entities that reside within realms other than our physical world.
Consequently, there is not a single portion of the volume, other than its In­
troduction (of which we still hold our doubts), that does not emanate from a
dubious source.
            As if Mr. Telano 1 s revelations are not sufficient to depreciate
much of the scientific knowledge mankind has gleaned during his many centuries
of existence, we are treated to an entree of various spirit "controls," the
most famous of which is none other than Mark.Probert•s female-toned male from
mankind's distant past, the 11 Yada Di Shi 1 ite. 11
            Each denizen from the II other side II uses his alloted time to poor
advantage by corroborating the others• tales, adding bits and pieces of his
own incomprehensible philosophy where deemed most appropriate. It would cer­
tainly be most unsporting of these creatures to assert their disapproval of
the spoutings of their contemporaries, even if what they are heard to expound
is in direct contradiction to their own alleged beliefs. However, one can al­
ways hope for an ideological war among spirits. It should prove quite inter­
esting!

TWENTY MINUTES OF TERROR
                             - by Jeanne Stevens -

 (Editor's�: Every year we receive dozens of personal accounts of saucer
 sightings from our readers. The one that follows was sent to us recently by a
 young woman who is sincerely trying to describe an event beyond her compre­
 hension. We present it with 'l minimum amount of editing.)
              I recently received a sample copy of your magazine after listening
 to you on the Long John radio show.
              The only encounter I have bad with a flying saucer was last sum­
 mer, while accompanied by my best friend. It was the most frightening and yet
 the most exciting experience of my entire life.
              Last August, my friend Marion Soderstrom invited me to spend the
 weekend at the estate of her uncle Emil Figge near Middletown, New York. We
 spent the afternoon wading in the creek and bird watching. We became so en­
 chanted with the loveliness of a clearing approximately l/4th mile from the
 main house, that we decided to camp out under the stars that night.
              We got together some blankets, canned fruit, and other supplies,
 including several orange candles that are specially treated so as to keep mos­
 quitoes away. Being a city girl, I was not used to seeing millions of stars in
 the sky. There was no moon, and it was quite eerie, with the croaking of the
 bullfrogs and the flickering of the candles.
              We were gaily chatting when suddenly we saw what we thought was a
 shooting star, except that.it kept coming closer and closer. Suddenly I became
 petrified. I thought perhaps it was a meteor, and would crash upon us. The ob­
 ject was very bright and had a white glow to it. It kept coming closer till it
 was only about 50 feet away. My heart was pounding in fear when it finally
 stopped. Then it simply hung suspended in the sky, and began to sway. We held
 each other in fear, for this was totally beyond our knowledge of things. The
 most terrifying thing of all was that it didn't make more than a slight hum.
 Even the intermittent sounds of the frogs were louder.
              Back and forth it swayed, undecided as to whether to go right or
 left or up or down. Then from its center there appeared a small orange light
 no bigger than a flashlight. This light began flickering in an odd sort of
 way. This continued for several minutes until we suddenly became aware of the
 fact that it was apparently flickering in imitation of our candles! We immedi­
 ately blew them out and scurried on our hands and knees into the low brush
 nearby. The most amazing thing of all was that as soon as the candles were ex­
 tinguished, the orange light on the saucer went out also!
              The object apparently could see us because of its own radiance,
 and it followed us for about 20 feet as we crawled desperately through the
 bushes. It was at this point that we could no longer maintain our composure.
 We fled in panic toward the main house, screaming to Marion's uncle for help.
              As I ran, I looked back for a moment and saw that the saucer was
 rising slowly, almost as if it was reluctant to leave. Then the humming sound
 from. the object suddenly became louder, almost like the noise of an electric
 motor. The strange orange light went on again, and the craft sped off toward
 the southwest, traveling faster than anything I had ever seen before. Within a
 couple of seconds it had disappeared completely from sight.
              Marion and I didn't spend the night under the stars after all. Our
 terrifying experience with the saucer made us decide that the safety of her
 uncle's house was far preferable to the uncertainties of the great outdoors.
 Even so, it was daybreak before we had calmed down enough to go to sleep, and
 ever since, I have been having occasional nightmares about that strange craft.

          � ELECTROMAGNETIC EFFECTS .QE FLITNG SAUCERS (Part Three)
                        - by John J. Robinson -
              I will now relate what I consider to be the most startling example
of the electromagnetic effect that has ever been recorded.
              At 2:00 a.m. on November 4th, 1957, during the height of the Lev­
elland 11 fiap" in the United States, a UFO appeared over the Brazilian fortress
of Itaipu. in the Brazilian state of Sao Vincente near the town of Santos. It
was a moonless tropical morning. The country was at peace, and the entire gar­
rison was sleeping with the exception of two sentinels who were on duty. These
two common soldiers were patrolling the ramparts. They were simple men and did
not expect anything except a routine tour of duty. It was later established
that they had never even heard of 11 platos voldados," or flying saucers.
              Their verbal report later stated that an orange glowing object ap­
proached until it was directly overhead. The object then slowly drifted down­
ward until it finally stopped about 180 feet above the gun turrets of the
fort. The two sentries watched in amazement as the huge disc-like object hov­
ered overhead. It looked like a misplaced over-bright moon. The UFO was as
large as a Douglas airplane, and the glow illuminated the top of the fort as
it hovered like a huge helicopter. The sound of a normal aircraft was not
heard, but the sentinels soon became aware of a strange humming sound which
obviously came from the object overhead.
             Suddenly the pitch of the sound changed to a whining type of noise.
As it did, the men were struck by an intolerable wave of heatt The heat seemed
to be a fire which was burning the clothes from them. As this heat engulfed
them, they screamed in agony. One man fell unconscious, while the other ran
screaming into the corner of one of the gun turrets. Falling beneath one of
the big guns, he lay moaning and muttering incoherently like a madman in pain.
              The screams awoke the garrison, which struggled awake only to pan­
ic anew, for nothing in the fort worked! Officers and men who started for
their battle stations in various stages of undress, since the lights did not
work, found that the electrical system of the fort malfunctioned also. All the
lights and communication systems had failed. Elevators and motors did not
function, and the guns could not be moved on their axes, nor could they be el­
evated. Powered doors to close off danger zones such as the rooms with explo­
sives did not operate, nor did the lifts to bring up shot and shell. The emer­
gency system was then resorted to. This was entirely independent of the regu­
lar source of electricity, and operated by motors which made the fort inde­
pendent of all other power sources. However, this emergency system _w �
lmtk either! The fort was helpless, at the mercy of the object which hung si­
lently, except for a slight hum, above it!
              Only one thing worked, and that, strangely enough, was the alarm
system, - which was not needed, as all the occupants of the helpless fort were
already alarmed enough without it. This alarm was set to go off at 5:00 a.m.,
but it began to ring with a strident clangor at 2:03 a.m., operated by power
from some totally inexplicable source. Everyone in the fort was in the grip of
fear - fear of the unknown force which had rendered this powerful modern fort­
ress helpless and ineffectual in a matter of a moment. The weapons of defense
were unusable, and nothing could be done when the attack came except to at­
tempt a last-ditch defense with hand weapons.
              Suddenly the UFO ascended straight up and left the scene of hope­
less helplessness in a sudden burst of speed. Abruptly the alarm stopped ring­
ing, the lights came on, the motors began functioning, and the auxiliary sys­
tem began to work at top speed. The panic took somewhat longer to abate.
              Orders were given to take the two sentries to the infirmary. There
both men were found to be suffering from an extreme state of shock, while on
their bodies was evidence of second degree burns. Fully ten percent of their
                                                                          11.

bodies were found to bear the marks of extreme heat, but the strangest thing
of all was that the portions of the body which were burned were those portions
protected by clothing. Yet, the cloth itself showed no evidence of heat!
            What could burn a man on the protected parts of his body without
affecting the exposed portions in any way except to irritate them? This was
the inexplicable question that the Brazilian military authorities were asked
to answer. This is the same strange and inexplicable question that UFO re­
searchers must try to answer! How can any type of electromagnetic effect do
this? Or is it a separate force of an entirely different sort?
            The suggestion has been made that ultra super-sonic waves might
have this ability, creating in matter a sound wave so violent that the matter
itself vibrates rapidly enough to set up a source of heat. If the burns were
received from the clothing in this manner, then we are dealing with a science
that can control and ascertain the vibratory rate of cloth within a few mo­
ments and apply the proper waves to such an object over a distance of 180
feet! Perhaps someday we will learn the correct answer to this baffling mys­
tery, but at present we only know the effect and not the cause!

                     FLYING SAUCERS�� UNITED NATIONS
                            - by Michael G. Mann -

(Editor's lisu&,: People have often asked why i� is that UFO's are never seen
over our large cities. The fact is that they sometimes are; but it seems that
city dwellers seldom look up·. In our March issue we presented the story of an
engineer who saw a fleet of flying saucers over Times Square back in 1937. The
following article, which is also a SAUCER NEWS exclusive, is written by an en­
gineer who has long been a student of the saucer enigma. It concerns a story
told to him by a co-worker who wishes to remain anonymous.)
            A few months ago, one of our company's field surveyors came to me
with an unusual yarn. At first I thought that he was just kidding, for that is
the practice among my friends who are aware of my interest in saucers. When I
accused him, and demanded the name of the person who put him up to playing a
joke on me, he became highly indignant. He finished his story, dropped a roll
of 120 film on my desk, and marched out of my office.
            A week ago I was in a camera store and discovered that I still had
that roll of film in my pocket. It was promptly placed in a processing enve­
lope. A couple of days later I picked up the film. I began to thumb through
the dozen or so prints. There was a poor shot of a little girl skipping rope,
some others of our construction site, and three of the surveyor. Because he
does not want his real name mentioned, I will call the surveyor Milton in this
article. It was the last two prints that caught my eye, and if I hadn't had
them developed myself, I never would have believed what I saw!
            Needless to say, I was in Milt's office early the next morning.
This time l was the one who was anxious to hear the story about the flying
saucer. It just wasn't my day, for I waited half the morning until the survey-

vantage. He had a full page of notes in his meticulously neat hand. My summary

                           WHENCE THE UF0 1 s?
                       - by N. S. Stringfellow -
             Many researchers and writers have taken the definite position that
fiying saucers are interplanetary. Major Donald·Keyhoe has done much painstak­
ing research and fascinating writing about UF0 1 s, and he seems very definitely
inclined to the interplanetary theory. In fact, he is so captivatingly persua­
sive that he is almost convincing. His would be a very satisfactory answer if
we could prove it, and make it stick. But, although the interplanetary hypoth­
esis is a strong one, there is much against it. I am sure that most of us
would� this to be the right answer to the saucer mystery, but is it? Let
us ex.amine the evidence.
             It appears that many of earth's scientists have little or no hope
of' any of the other planets in our solar system being inhabited. This has been
a great disappointment to some of us, as we can reasonably hope to achieve in­
terplanetary travel within our own solar system within the lifetime of most of
us. Travel to the stars is another matter, however.
             The great astronomer Harlow Shapley, in his interesting little
book "Of Stars and Men," suggests that there must be at least 100,000 •1ife­
bearing planets in our own galaxy. This estimate seems very reasonable. Many
of earth's scientists seemed very optimistic about space travel early in the
space programs of the United States and Russia, but now some are seriously
doubting whether earth men or forms of life on any planet will!!!!: be able to
bridge the staggering distances between the stars. They tell us that our near­
est star, Proxima in Centaurus, is four light years away. A light year is the
distance light travels in a year at the fantastic rate of 186,000 miles per
second. Can, we imagine space vehicles traveling down all those light years?
Reliable accounts of the UF0 1 s indicate that they travel at great speeds. With
their admitted great speeds, how long would it take to travel four times six
thousand billion miles at, for example, fifty thousand miles per hour? Figure
14.
it out for yourself. The answer is more impressive that way.
            We can imagine a spacecraft being prepared for a week's journey,
or even a month's or a year's; but it is hard to imagine one prepared for a
longer journey than that. Some observers have estimated very high speeds for
saucers. Mr. Kenneth Arnold of Idaho estimated 1200 to 1800 miles per hour for
the chain of nine saucers he saw over Mt. Rainier in 1947. This might not have
been their maximum speed, but if it was, try dividing twenty-four thousand
billion by 1200, etc., to get an idea of how long it would take for these
craft to travel to the earth from a planet in orbit around Proxima.
            Some people have even suggested that the UFO's come from a planet
in some other galaxy-. Our nearest galaxy neighbors, the Magellanic Clouds, are
about 150,000 light years away, and the next in distance is the Great Nebula
in Andromeda, about two million light years away. To travel such distances in
a lifetime (any human's, animal's, entity's, or monster's lifetime) would re­
quire unbelievable speeds. The mere thought of such speeds takes us far afield
into fiction and fantasy. Even assuming that we earthlings might develop
speeds as high as 50,000 miles per hour, we still wouldn't get anywhere!
            Distance is not the only deterrent to interplanetary travel. There
is also the asteroid belt in our own solar system. That zone is between the
planets Mars and Jupiter, and is full of unshapen orbiting bodies. The larger
of these bodies are from 120 to 480 miles in diameter, whereas the average di­
ameter is 15 to 20 miles. More than 2800 of them are large enough to be iden­
tified, and more than 1500 have been catalogued. Many of these asteroids are a
mile or less in their largest dimension. Think of how many there may be from
an inch to 100 feet across. One of even a small diameter could do terrible
damage to a spacecraft in collision. Nothing has ever been sent out from the
earth through the asteroid zone and returned to earth. Reliable accounts of
UFO's describe them as being larger than our largest planes, and we can assume
them to be rather durably made; but are they durable enough to come safely
through the dangers of the asteroid belt? I doubt it!
            I have a personal theory about our moon. I believe it is a little
burned-out sun. At times during daylight it appears pretty rough. In binocu­
lars and small telescopes at night, this roughness is amplified. A study of a
portion of our moon blown up from a large telescopic picture shows just how
rough and pockmarked it is. My private theory is that when the Great Architect
of the      Universe
brought order to                                                                        _.~
this earth and de­
cided it needed a                                                      •

ets in other galaxies, and there are probably many bodies, from sand-grain
size to boulder and larger sizes, out in space beyond our solar system.
            Another hazard is the Van Allen radiation belt. We don't know that
a manned earth craft could successfully go through this field, nor do we know
that a craft from any other planet could successfully pass through it to come
to earth. We may be quarantined to within 600 ·or 1000 miles of our own planet
until the end of time.
            Discouraging? Yes, and until man or other life forms out in space
learn to travel as fast or nearly as fast as light, can build spacecraft as
durable or almost as durable as the moon, and can find a way to power them
better and cheaper than anything that has been tried on earth to date, we will
just have to give up our cherished dream of interplanetary travel. We must
realize the limitations of poor little Homo Sapiens, and we must realize that
similar life forms on other planets out in space must have their limitations
too. It is no wonder that so many good saucer researchers have given up and
turned to the study of psychic phenomenal
            Whence the UF0 1 s? We are forced to look closer to home in our
search. Someone has suggested a planet like our earth, in the same orbit and
on the other side of the sun, that has not been discovered yet. Others have
suggested the moon. It has been pointed out that there are definite signs of
life there. The shadow of a bridge has been seen where no shadow or bridge was
ever seen before, and there have even been dome-like structures seen on the
moon! With binoculars we can see bright spots on the moon, and we can see what
appears to be beams or channels eminating from them - particularly from the
crater called Tyco. Some people believe that life could comfortably exist
there in spite of the extreme temperatures. We know that one side of the moon
is hidden from us, and we cannot be sure what might be there. We have seen
an alleged picture of the dark side of the moon, but it shows nothing, as it
is far inferior to telescopic pictures of the exposed side taken from earth.
It is reasonable to believe that in a very short time we will find out defi­
nitely whether or not our nearest neighbor in space contains intelligent life.
            Another theory is that there are holes in the poles of our planet.
Saucer activity and other strange phenomena have been observed in the polar
regions, especially in the ·southern one. Other people believe that there are
strange forms of intelligent life living in the sea, in the earth's caverns,
or even deep down in the bowels of our planet.
            Whence the UF0 1 s? I cannot give the correct answer any more than
you can; but couldn't they be coming from somewhere within about 240,000 miles
of your home?

                 RECENT NEWS
             EAST COAST SAUCER CONVENTION PLANNED FOR LATE SUMMER: Plans are
 underway for a gigantic East Coast saucer convention to be held near New York
 City this August, simultaneously with the New York World's Fair. This would be
 the first such convention in this area since Howard Manger's, back in 1958. As
 of this writing, no site has yet been decided upon. However, a committee has
 been set up, headed by Lee Medeville, a researcher living on Long Island.
 Other members of the committee include Wayne Aho, Harry Hoffman, and your Edi­
 tor, who is in charge of publicity. When further details develop we will let
 our readers know, even if this means sending out one or more special bulletins
 to our entire mailing list. As the convention date grows nearer, publicity on
 the Long John Show as well as other radio programs is anticipated.
             WORLD-FAMOUS PSYCHIC FACES JAIL SENTENCE: On February 10th, the
 noted psychic Peter Herkos was arrested . for allegedly having impersonated an
 FBI agent the previous December. Herkos is supposed to have told a gas station
 attendant in Wisconsin that he was the FBI man who solved the slaying of Pres­
 ident Kennedy, and that he was on his way to Las Vegas to investigate the kid­
 napping of Frank Sinatra Jr. On March 3rd, Herkos was indicted by a federal
 grand jury. He now faces a possible three year prison term. During his fascin­
 ating career, Herkos has aided police in solving several murders, although at
 times his psychic impressions have been inaccurate, and on one occasion his
 information led to the arrest of a suspect who was later found to be innocent.
               INTERVIEW WITH DR. DONALD MENZEL AND BOSTON
               RADIO APPEARANCE BY SAUCER NEWS EDITOR:
               On February 25th, your Editor made a brief trip to Boston to ap­
 pear on the Bob Kennedy Contact Show,a program heard nightly from 6:30 to 8:00
 p.m. on Station WBZ (50,000 watts.) Whil.e in town, we set up a brief interview
 with the arch-enemy of Saucerdom, Dr. Donald H. Menzel, author of the recent
 book 11 The World of Flying Saucers� reviewed in the March issue of SAUCER NEWS.
 The interview was held in Dr. Menzel's office at the Harvard Observatory in
 Cambridge, and was witnessed by saucer researcher Robert Forrest, who had set
 up the WBZ appearance for us.
               It is not possible to come to a firm conclusion about Dr. Menzel's
 personality from one short meeting, but our impression is that Richard E. Wal­
 lace, who wrote the review for us, was quite correct in stating that Menzel
 does not write his anti-saucer books for profit, fame, or prestige. It is
 fairly obvious that his private war against flying saucers is a vendetta car­
 ried on for reasons best known to himself. Dr. Menzel was reasonably cordial,
 but tended to answer specific questions in generalities, much as the Air Force
 has been accused of doing.
               The world-famous astrophysicist informed us that he "might" listen
 in to the Bob Kennedy Show that evening. The program takes many phone calls
 from listeners, and as it turned out, the most persistent caller of all was Dr.
 Menzel, who achieved about 10 minutes or air time out of the hour and a half
 program. When cornered in the debate, he resorted to generalities as he had
 done in the face-to-face interview. All telephone calls taken over the air,
 except for the one from Menzel, were limited to one or two minutes.
               A man called the station shortly after Menzel finally got off the
 line, and asked about the rumor that Jonathan Swift, author of the 18th Cen­
 tury classic "Gulliver's Travels, 11 was actually a visitor from Mars. We re­
 plied that our onlf confirmation of this rumor (as was pointed out in Mr. Wal­
 lace's book review) is an article in: the February, 1964 issue of "Worlds of
 Tomorrow," written by Dr. Menzel 1 s co-author, Mrs. Lyle G. Boyd. Mrs. Boyd,
who is a science-fiction writer by trade, speculated in a factual article in
"Worlds of Tomorrow" that Jonathan Swift might have been a Martian. Yet, when
co-authoring "The World of F1.ying Saucers" with Dr. Menzel, she was unable to
accept even the much less fantastic possibility that UFO's could be real!
            The Bob Kennedy Contact Show was an extremely interesting and
highly rewarding experience for your Editor. It is a well-run effort, with a
M. C. who is both genial and highly skilled. As a result of the program, more
than 300 people wrote in for sample copies of SAUCER NEWS, from a radius of
almost 500 miles from Boston. We hope to be on the show again shortly, talking
about flying saucers and possibly treasure hunting, as well.
            NEWS BRIEFS: On the night of December 5th, two high school girls
in Cavalier, North Dakota, were terrified by a UFO which circled their car at
high speed while they were driving home. The object was at low altitude, and
its oval shape could be seen quite clearly. It was a bright orange in color,
and was surrounded by a light haze. The girls, named Nita Main and Cherly
Kelm, said that the UFO sped across the front of their automobile and circled
it to the side before disappearing to the southeast••••On New Years Eve, lis­
teners in Denmark, Sweden, and Norway who believed that a fictional radio de­
scription of a space invasion was the real thing, jammed the telephone switch­
board of the Danish Broadcasting Corporation. Some asked for confirmation that
a spaceship had landed, and others, realizing the program was fiction, com­
plained that it was too realistic and had frightened their children. The whole
event was reminiscent of the Orsen Welles "War of the Worlds" scare in 1938•••
At 6:30 p.m. on February 5th, a blazing object flashed across the skies and
was seen by hundreds of startled witnesses in Washington, Oregon, and Montana.
A pilot for West Coast Airlines saw the object coming toward him and was
tempted to dodge it, though it apparently passed him at a considerable dis­
tance. The UFO, thought by some to be a meteor, is reported to have disinte­
grated or crashed. An attempt was made by the American Meteor Society to lo­
cate it••••
            The Russian news agency Tass claims that the Russians have devised
a linguistic key for deciphering unknown texts. No knowledge is required of
the language in which the text was written. Helped by an electronic computer,
this key can analyze mathematically any language - even Martian - and under­
stand it•••••Scientists have spotted nine colossal gas masses on the edge of
the Universe, 3,000 million light years away. This gives confirmation to the
theory that the Universe had no beginning, because matter is constantly being
created. The alternative theory is that the Universe began with a "big bang"
about 10,000 million light years ago •••• The Tokyo Astronomical Observatory has
stated that the revolution of the earth has inexplicably become faster since
the end of 1963, and that this will probably cause abnormal weather this com­
ing summer.

            SAUCER NEWS ATTENDS A SEANCE: On the night of March 7th, Sandy
Moseley attended a ouija board seance at the home of Don and Joan Ma.rowski, in
New York's Greenwich Village section. Also present were Long John regulars
Stewart Robb and Kaigh Deigh, as well as several other people. Messages were
received from Aldous Huxley and others; but the most interesting to us was one
relating to a 24-year-old girl we know who recently engaged in a double sui­
cide with her boy friend. The two people operating the ouija board at the time
were, at the very least, successfully demonstrating telepathy, for the name of
this dead girl came through although no one in the room knew it except Sandy.
Next the name of the girl's dog came out. The alleged spirit of the dead girl
insisted that she had not really killed herself but had been killed by her boy
friend. A man's name, unfamiliar to Sandy, was then given by the spirit. Sandy
phoned the dead woman's mother to find out if this was the name of her daugh-
18.
ter•s companion. The mother became very upset and refused to give any info:rm.a­
tion, so apparently the name had some meaning to her. - Other strange examples
of telepathy or co-incidence occurred th.at night, and we intend to go to more
seances in an att8lllpt to learn more about this intriguing field of research.
            FURTHER RADIO APPEARANCES BY SAUCER NEWS STAFF: On March 6th, your
Editor went on the Bob Zanotti Show again (WFMU in New Jersey), accompanied by
our Advertising Manager Eugene Steinberg. This time the hour and a half dis­
cussion concerned treasure hunting as well as recent events in the saucer
field. - On the morning of March 21st, your Editor appeared on the Long John
Show with George Adamski and others, discussing Adamski's controversial con­
tact claims. Mr. Adamski was in New York to give two lectures for the NYSIB
group.

            FORTEAN ITF}{Sa On Christmas eve, a chicken in Biarritz, France,
which had strayed away from its farm, delighted its owners by coming back and
laying a giant egg weighing eight ounces•••••Witchcraft, one of the pre-Chris­
tian religions of England, is still practiced there in a highly organized man­
ner, according to a recent report from London. There are said to be at least
six covens (or groups) in the country, each composed of 13 wi.tches,who confine
themselves to the practice of white rather than black magic. Sabbats (or meet­
ings) are held in the woods four times a year, with secret rituals which ap­
parently involve animal sacrifices•••••There 1 s also a group in England called
the Panacea Society, which claims to have the prescription for all the world's
ills. The message was sealed in a box by a prophetess named Joanne Southcott
before her death in 1841. Ever since then, the Society has been clamoring for
the box to be opened; but according to the rules set forth by the prophetess,
it can be opened only in time of crisis by two dozen bishops of the Church of
England surrounded by a multitude of believers. To date, these conditions have
never been met. The location of the box is known only to the Society, and its
contents are known to no one ••••
            Soviet scientist Boris F. Porshinev recently stated that he be­
lieves the controversial Abominable Snowmen of the Himalayas are survivers of
Neanderthal man, who dominat.ed western Europe 75,000 to 25,000 years ago. Dur­
ing recent years, Abominable Sno'Wl!len have been seen by several scientists on
expeditions to the Himalayas. Their footprints have been photographed and pre­
served, but no Sno'Wl!lan has ever been captured or photographed••••Cancer causes
60� of the deaths in the village of Samford, Devon, England. This is supposed­
ly due to the unusually high concentrations of radioactivity in lettuce which
is grown locally•••••A sea serpent of some ·sort washed up on a beach at Oak
Harbor, Washington, last October, and caused a great deal of controversy as to
its species. Some observers wrote it off as a whale, but as in most such
cases, the true explanation was not so simple. Many curiosity seekers and oth­
ers carted away sections of the decomposed creature, which was 25 feet long
and had a head similar to a horse's. The head was "stolen" by a local high
school teacher, who was not able to definitely identify it as belonging to any
known fish. The teacher buried the head pending the arrival of experts from
the University of Washington who were expected to continue the study; but we
have no report on their findings, if any••••
            A 105-foot Navy PT boat, converted into a pleasure craft, struck
an unidentified object in the water 25 miles off the coast of California last
February 5th, and sank. The eleven persons on board were rescued by a Coast
Guard helicopter. R. w. Rutherford, the man who had chartered the boat; said:
"We struck an unseen object. It was not a log. It was like metal. It hit the
bow first and then took away our rudder. I wouldn't want to say anything for
publication as to what it was, but it definitely was a strange object."••••
Farris Bryant, Governor of Florida, recently sent a small boy a letter con-
                                                                           19.
firming the fact that it sometimes rains frogs and tiny fish in his state. The
letter was in answer to an inquiry by a fifth grade student •••Air Force scien­
tists are still studying mysterious red and pink spots on the moon, which are
regarded with skepticism by other scientists who have not seen the spots. The
Air Force moon watchers hope to get photographs in June to prove their point.
(Isn't it an interesting switch for the Air Force to see something that other
people don•t believel? - Editor.) ••••A potato weighing 4½ pounds was harvested
in Barco de Avila, Spain, last November•••Two couples playing bridge last Feb­
ruary ended up with cards dealt in such a way that each player received 13
cards of the same suit. One player had shuffled, another had cut the cards,
and a third had dealt them. The odds against this happening are figured at 153
million to onel •••••A cat sanehow followed a family that moved from Miami,
Florida, to Harrison, Ohio, although the animal had disappeared in Miami two
months before the move, and had never been to Harrison. The family is convinc­
ed that the cat which mysteriously showed up at their new home in Ohio is the
same one they lost in Florida, because it looks and acts exactly the same,
and it even gets along with their dog� just as the cat in Miami did.
            GASOLINE MADE FRCM WATER7777 An Illinois man named Guido Franch
claims to have discovered a secret formula derived from coal, which when added
to water, will perform better than conventional gasoline. The cost is only
about two cents per gallon. If this claim is true, it is hard to understand
why Franch has spent many years trying to sell the formula without success. To
make the matter even more mysterious, Franch claims to have obtained the for­
mula from a scientist named Alexander Kraft - now dead - who came over from
Germany after World War I and invented a flying saucer which still exists in a
secret abandoned coal mine in Illinois, guarded by a mystery cult of some
sort. The saucer runs on this mixture, but it also has electromagnetic en­
gines, used for traveling above the earth's atmosphere. Kraft's craft has made
many successful test runs over the years, according to the story, but inex­
plicable obstacles make it even harder to sell than the fuel. - Part of the
above information is from the DuPage County, Illinois "Daily Journal," of De­
cember 11th, 1963, and part is from a SAUCER NEWS subscriber who visited our
Headquarters recently and gave us a small bottle of the fuel mixture to have
analyzed. We hope to have further news about all this in our next issue!
